## HW-01 Session Material PDF Preview HTTP 500 (November 24, 2026)

- Confirmed request: `GET /api/case-sessions/f98026d3-378a-451e-a545-91e263acf869/materials-pdf` against linked DEV `lctkqjjkhpyootwvttvj`. The requested Case Session is active, actual/template sequence 2, with a pinned snapshot of six ordered blocks: four rich-text pages, one video, and one audio resource. No Homework answers or counseling notes were queried.
- The local Next log did not contain the specific request; the only nearby server errors were unrelated “destination stream closed early” entries. Reproducing the PDF generator directly against this exact DEV snapshot produced the actual exception: `Session text contains an invalid link`, thrown by `assertRichTextSupported` while validating a persisted TipTap `link` mark.
- Root cause: five link marks in the first pinned rich-text block have no `attrs.href`. These legacy/dangling link marks are text formatting with no usable destination; they should render as ordinary text, not invalidate the entire PDF. A read-only DEV aggregate found 30 missing-href link marks across three of nine snapshots with links, so this is a pre-existing snapshot compatibility issue beyond this one Session, not an HW-01 regression (the HW-01 diff does not change the PDF route, loader, renderer, or snapshot model).
- Minimal fix: rich-text link marks with a missing/null/blank destination are accepted as non-clickable text; non-empty unsafe destinations still fail closed. The renderer already adds a clickable PDF link only when `safeHttpUrl()` accepts the href, so no unsafe target is emitted. Snapshot bytes/order, access authorization, PDF styling, and export format are unchanged.
- Regression coverage was added for a dangling link mark preserving its text without a PDF URI, and for rejecting a non-empty unsafe rich-text URL. Focused PDF renderer/loader/route tests pass **19/19**. A temporary, uncommitted read-only DEV snapshot smoke test passed and generated a valid 18,303-byte PDF from all six pinned blocks; the temporary test file was removed.
- Preview and Download share the same API generator; only `Content-Disposition` differs. Route tests verify identical PDF bytes for inline Preview and attachment Download and correct inline/attachment headers. A direct authenticated local browser request could not be performed: no authenticated browser session is available, and unauthenticated localhost access correctly returned 404. Therefore affected-session authenticated Preview/Download remain manual browser checks, not claimed as passed.
- Full Vitest suite passes **1,563/1,563 across 271 files**; typecheck, lint, production build, and `git diff --check` pass. No database writes, migration, notification, snapshot change, merge, or deployment occurred. The PDF fix and this state update are committed and pushed on `feature/hw-01-multiple-homework-pages`; do not merge/deploy until separately approved. Preserve the unrelated `.env.example` and `AGENTS.md` edits and every existing stash entry.

### Session Material page-navigation refinement (November 24, 2026)

- **Implementation:** Homework authoring now supports titled pages, selection, add/rename/reorder/delete, and moving an existing content block through its contextual menu. Only the selected page's blocks appear in the editor. Deleting a page warns that its page/content will be permanently removed; the client requires exact uppercase `DELETE`, and the save RPC compares the deleted IDs with the persisted draft manifest before accepting the operation. Draft saves allow incomplete/untitled/empty pages; publishing enforces a nonempty title and at least one block for every page in addition to the existing block/question and Side-by-Side validation.
- **Participant/review experience:** Couple Homework displays the current page title and `Page n of m`, with Previous/Next controls. Answers remain in the existing all-block state while page content is filtered, draft save and final validation still cover the full Homework, and optional questions remain optional. Counselor review groups answers by page. Builder preview renders each page heading and its blocks. Legacy versions with no manifest/placement are treated as one implicit Page 1; existing version/block/answer IDs and saved historical snapshots are not backfilled or rewritten. Editing a published version creates an immutable-preserving draft clone, including its page metadata and block placement.
- **Database:** Added unapplied migration `supabase/migrations/20261124163708_homework_pages.sql` with nullable `homework_versions.page_manifest` and `homework_version_blocks.page_id`, an authenticated atomic page-aware authoring wrapper, publish page validation, Counselor review page metadata, and published-version/block immutability triggers. Existing authoring and review authorization contracts are retained. The wrapper also carries and persists the established `is_final` Session designation. The migration contains no data backfill; legacy versions retain null page metadata and are presented as implicit Page 1. The current DEV schema was confirmed unchanged after the attempted transaction: the migration remains pending, both added columns are absent, and the aggregate counts of Homework versions, version blocks, participant progress, and answers match preflight. No actual Couple submission was touched.

## UI-07 Coach/Counselor Case Page Redesign (November 23, 2026)

- **Implementation:** The shared Coach/Counselor Case detail page now keeps the Couple identity/photos, counseling status, current Session, and upcoming meeting visible above four accessible segmented Radix tabs: Session (default), Homework, Meetings & Reminders, and Settings. The compact right rail persists Counseling Progress, Assessments, Prepare & Enrich, and Case Actions (including Complete Program). The Case rail opts into compact Assessment summaries: heading, assessment title, aggregate submission count, Required badge where applicable, and the existing authorized View Results link; partner-level results remain available only inside View Results. Other shared-card consumers retain their existing detailed presentation. Existing Case authorization, query paths, lifecycle actions, meeting scheduling, assessment management, material export, notification status/realtime, and Prepare & Enrich behavior are reused; no schema, RPC, permission, Homework submission, or notification changes were made. The Sessions listing also uses equal-height implicit grid rows, flexible card columns, matching minimum space for variable-length Couple names and Session descriptions, and bottom-anchored Next Meeting/Progress rows. Oversight cards use a dedicated grid separate from the hierarchy rail, shared by Campus Lead and Coach views; Counselor cards use the same card layout.
- **Session navigation and Homework:** Session highlights the current Case Session and lists each existing Session Material PDF with its Session title and existing Preview/Download control. Current partner Homework statuses appear as a compact summary. Homework tab defaults to the active Session (or the latest available Session when no active one exists), places the current Session first in the selector, and supports selecting historical Sessions for their existing saved statuses and authorized answer review. Selection is local display state only and does not change actual Case Session progression. Reopen remains available only for a submitted partner on the actual active Session when the existing `canReopenHomework` authorization and active Case/Session conditions hold; historical Sessions never expose Reopen.
- **Meetings and Settings:** Meetings & Reminders displays the upcoming meeting and existing meeting history, plus the existing authorized invitation/reminder delivery status and resend controls. Existing Case Actions remain in the persistent rail. Settings reuses the existing Assessment schedule/session assignment component only for callers with its existing schedule permission; unavailable data/permissions receive explanatory empty states. No new configuration options or permissions were added.

## S8F Paired Response Parser Correction and DEV Verification (November 16, 2026)

- **DEV parser defect corrected:** `private.homework_side_by_side_answer_column(value text)` previously selected an unqualified `value` from `jsonb_array_elements(... ) AS rows(value)`, colliding with its PL/pgSQL input argument. The function's exception handler converted this ambiguity into `NULL`, causing valid nonempty paired-response payloads to be rejected. Added the forward-only migration `20261116130000_homework_paired_response_parser_alias.sql`, using the explicit `row_entry(element)` alias and preserving the signature, immutable/search-path settings, validation, grants, and existing answer format. The earlier applied migration was not edited.
- Confirmed linked Supabase DEV (`lctkqjjkhpyootwvttvj`) and that the corrective migration alone was pending. Applied only that migration. Post-apply migration listing aligns and linked dry-run reports up to date; `npx supabase db lint --linked --level error` reports no findings. A rollback-only parser SQL test passes valid nonempty/multiline rows and rejects duplicate IDs, malformed UUIDs, and non-string answer values.
- Added `supabase/tests/homework_paired_response_runtime.sql`, a rollback-only authenticated-role verifier using ephemeral synthetic Auth, Couple, Case, Session, and Homework rows. It passed valid two-pair payload persistence, multiline preservation, draft persistence, required partial-pair rejection, required submission, optional blank pairs, independent partner persistence, and cross-partner progress denial. A follow-up read-only check found zero leftover synthetic verifier profiles. Authorization metadata confirms `anon` cannot execute the public persistence RPC, `authenticated` can, and the private implementation is not executable by `authenticated`.
- At S8F, Counselor review/reopen and the unassigned Counselor boundary had not yet been verified; S8G below supersedes that open status. The broad Homework foundation verifier also had a stale expected-error assertion for a `rich_text` fixture, corrected in S8G without changing production validation.

## Secure Photo Handoff Upload Concurrency (November 13, 2026)

- Confirmed the race: simultaneous requests could both validate one active token and upload with `upsert` to the same canonical profile-photo object before the completion RPC serialized token use. A late request could overwrite bytes after another request had already succeeded.
- Added migration `20261113100000_secure_photo_handoff_uploads.sql`, applied only to verified Supabase DEV `lctkqjjkhpyootwvttvj`. Claims are serialized while locking the onboarding profile and handoff row; each claim writes to a unique private `profiles/{profile}/handoffs/{attempt}.avif` path without upsert. A finalization RPC atomically publishes only the current, unexpired attempt when the profile is still onboarding and its photo pointer matches the claim snapshot. Failed or superseded attempts can be released/retried and are removed only after checking no profile points at the object. A two-minute claim lease allows recovery after an abandoned request. Handoff-specific RPCs are service-role-only; existing Storage privacy remains, with narrowly scoped authenticated reads for a user's own handoff object. The previous direct completion RPC is revoked. Existing canonical desktop uploads remain unchanged.
- Updated onboarding completion validation to accept either the existing canonical avatar object or an existing completed handoff object linked to that profile. Desktop polling now signs the authoritative `profiles.photo_path` after completion, so it displays the uniquely published object. Handoff pages and API responses are forced dynamic, uncached, and `no-referrer`; unavailable/valid handoff behavior and the camera workflow remain unchanged.
- Added focused unit/migration/route/page tests and expanded `npm run verify:photo-handoff` to create and clean synthetic DEV users. Actual parallel HTTP uploads against local production-mode application code and DEV proved exactly one successful publication, winner-image integrity, token reuse denial, failed-upload retry, replacement invalidation during an in-flight attempt, expired-token denial, profile isolation, and synthetic auth/profile cleanup. The final integration run passed. No ministry user or real Couple record was modified. The user subsequently confirmed the secure handoff flow after the concurrency fix; Android device testing remains deferred.
- Validation passed: 21 focused tests across six files, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, linked DB lint (existing unrelated warnings only), `supabase db push --linked --dry-run` (DEV history up to date), and `git diff --check`. The first integration-verifier attempt exposed assertion assumptions about the database's safe false/released result for a replaced claim; the verifier was corrected, and the final parallel DEV run passed.
- Implementation commit `51d34a877393c5ede884dfff174d536c260e9058` was fast-forward merged and pushed to `origin/main`. Dedicated Vercel project `resonate-movement/marriage-ministry-dev` (`prj_J5QbuVHBVRyQeq78Qd6G9HmJFbVt`) is connected to `resonatemovement/marriage_ministry`; deployment `dpl_2PJ39scuTDBvLF8KxwmGzSZYRMWA` reached `READY` at that exact `main` SHA. Vercel labels this target `production` within the dedicated DEV project. Actual Resonate PROD was not accessed. At the time of this concurrency release, the onboarding phone-handoff entry button was still visible; its separately approved follow-up was implemented in commit `47cbc2bf1155bd71d3d0ad6a488175ff81e42523`. The feature branch was merged and deleted normally; all seven stashes remain preserved. Migration rollback would require a separately reviewed forward correction because the migration has already been applied to DEV; do not drop or remove profile-photo paths that are active. Remaining risk: if a request process terminates after Storage accepts a unique object but before the route's cleanup runs, the object remains private and unpublished; it is removed on the next retry/replacement cleanup, with no automatic background garbage collector introduced. Physical phone testing, Android device testing, and background cleanup were not performed.

## Native Phone Camera Capture (November 10, 2026)

- Added a separate hidden `image/*` file input with `capture="user"` and a clearly labeled “Use Phone Camera” action in the ready, live camera, and captured-preview states. This requests the browser's supported camera-capture experience; it does not guarantee launch of Apple's standalone Camera app or identical image processing. The existing browser camera, Flip Camera, and Photo Library remain available.
- Native and library selections use the same existing file-change listener, source validation, preview, confirmation, and authorized upload flow. The input value is cleared on each change for repeat selections. Choosing native capture stops any active browser stream and returns the UI to a ready state first; a canceled capture leaves the existing preview (if any) intact and does not leave a loading state. Upload remains user-confirmed and protected by the existing immediate duplicate guard.
- HEIC/HEIF is not converted or accepted by the current upload source validator (JPG, PNG, WebP, and AVIF only); native files in those formats display the existing supported-format error. No storage, upload endpoint, schema, token, URL, polling, or authorization changes.

## Phone Photo Handoff Camera Quality (November 10, 2026)

- Audited the camera preview and capture path. Neither the live `<video>` nor canvas capture had a horizontal flip; capture draws the frame at its native `videoWidth`/`videoHeight`. The preview now explicitly uses `transform-none`, and canvas capture explicitly resets to the identity transform before drawing, preserving natural orientation and aspect ratio. The profile-photo library path was not changed.
- Added non-mandatory 1920×1440 resolution preferences alongside the existing front/rear `facingMode`; these are ideal hints, not exact constraints, so browsers may select supported modes. No aspect-ratio constraint, crop, lens correction, image conversion, upload, handoff, or authorization behavior changed.
- Focused phone camera and capture-view tests pass (18 tests), covering default/front and rear selection, switching/recovery, preferred constraints, native canvas dimensions, identity transform, live and captured preview states, and existing controls. `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, `npm run typecheck` (rerun after the build generated `.next/types`), and `git diff --check` pass. Typecheck initially ran concurrently with the build and encountered transient missing `.next/types`; the sequential rerun passed. No physical-device/browser camera verification was performed.
- Root cause evidence: there was no application-authored mirroring transform to remove. Whether the reported mirroring originates in a specific browser/device camera stream remains unverified. Close-up curvature is likely phone front-lens perspective/wide-angle distortion, not layout or capture stretching; browser constraints cannot remove optical distortion.
- Feature commit `444908c45bbf3d3e4b2d3413e4dd2be78dbc4d4e` was fast-forward merged and pushed to `origin/main`. ## Intake Referral Workflow (November 9, 2026)

- Added a distinct `referred_elsewhere` Intake Request status. Admin/Super Admin can refer requests globally; Campus Leads can refer and reopen only within their assigned active campuses. Coaches, Counselors, Couples, anonymous callers, and cross-campus Campus Lead actions are denied by the database authorization boundary. The mismatched server action now admits Admin and Campus Lead workspaces, while Couple invitations remain Admin/Super Admin-only and are hidden from Campus Lead controls.
- `Refer Elsewhere` is available during active pre-invitation review (`Ready for Review` and `Under Review`). It requires a trimmed 10–500 character free-text reason and confirmation. Database validation independently enforces the length. The reason, actor, and timestamp are recorded in existing Intake status history and audit events; no destination field, column, table, or email was added. Existing `Close Request` reasons—including historical “Referred to professional”—and Counseling Case referral behavior are unchanged.
- Referred requests cannot enter the invitation flow. Reopening uses the existing `Reopen for Review` action to return to `Under Review`, preserving referral history and creating a fresh status-entry timestamp. The legacy generic status RPC cannot bypass this reopen action. Referred requests are explicitly excluded from Intake alert detection; reopening makes them eligible under existing thresholds. The existing `all` Intake view/status filter includes the new state, while `open` and `added` retain their prior meanings. Request details display the historical referral reason.
- The Dashboard `Other Outcomes` section now includes a separate `Intake Referrals` tile labeled in Intake Requests, distinct from the existing Case `Referred` Couple outcome. New read-only `get_dashboard_intake_referral_count()` returns only an aggregate for Admin/Super Admin globally or Campus Leads within active assigned campuses; existing four summary cards and Session presentation are unchanged.
- Forward migrations `20261109130000_intake_referral_status.sql`, `20261109140000_intake_referral_actions_and_alerts.sql`, and `20261109150000_dashboard_intake_referral_count.sql` were preflighted and applied only to verified Supabase DEV `lctkqjjkhpyootwvttvj`. Remote/local migration history is aligned through `20261109150000`; generated database types were refreshed from DEV. No existing Intake, Couple, Case, or assignment data was changed. Authenticated DEV verifier fixtures were removed and checked after testing.

## Intake Alert Settings & Detection — Phase 3A, Task 1 (November 5, 2026)

- Added the Admin Settings `Intake Request Alerts` section with four persisted whole-hour thresholds: Ready for Review 48, Under Review 72, Ready to Invite 168, and Invited with incomplete onboarding 168. Inputs enforce integer values from 1–720 with inline validation; saving is disabled unless valid changes exist. Missing settings cause the Settings page to report unavailable instead of using runtime defaults.
- Added migration `20261105100000_intake_alert_settings_and_detection.sql` and applied only that migration to verified Supabase DEV project `lctkqjjkhpyootwvttvj`. Migration history is aligned through `20261105100000`. Admin/Super Admin settings writes use a role-checked SECURITY DEFINER RPC. The reusable alert RPC allows Admin/Super Admin global scope and limits Campus Leads to active assigned campuses; Campus Leads cannot read the settings table or update thresholds.
- Detection uses latest matching status-history `changed_at` and elapsed hours, never `updated_at`. Ready for Review appears as actionable immediately and changes to stalled after threshold; Under Review and Ready to Invite require threshold age; Invited requires threshold age, two distinct linked invitations, and at least one partner without both accepted invitation and completed profile onboarding. It returns only incomplete partner names, returns at most one current alert per request, omits Closed, and fails closed for missing status history. No notification records, jobs, email, Dashboard UI, or lifecycle changes were added.
- Authenticated DEV verification passed with existing Admin, Super Admin, Campus Lead, Couple, and anonymous identities: global staff reads match; Campus Lead scope is limited and cross-campus requests are denied; Admin and Super Admin writes pass; Campus Lead writes and direct settings-table reads are denied; unauthenticated and Couple reads are denied; invalid database thresholds are rejected without changing stored values. The verifier removed its 11 temporary Intake Requests, 3 temporary Couple groups, 6 Invitations, and associated history; original threshold settings were restored.

Scheduling sheets use concise helper copy. Scheduling and rescheduling now reject dates before the current `America/Los_Angeles` date and reject today's times at or before the current instant; the optional DatePicker minimum is applied only to lifecycle scheduling, while manual MM/DD/YYYY entry remains supported. The server action converts canonical `HH:mm` to an instant using explicit Los Angeles timezone rules, and a database execution-time trigger rejects past starts on Meeting inserts or reschedules. Shared DropdownMenuItem supplies pointer cursor, warm-neutral hover and Radix keyboard-highlight/focus-visible surfaces; disabled items retain not-allowed treatment without interactive hover, while destructive items retain danger text and subtle danger surfaces. Separators and labels remain non-interactive. The separate Current Session, Current Homework, and Next Meeting cards were removed. Homework and Meeting Invites headings use `text-brand-secondary`; a balanced 24px gap above and below the divider centers it between the Homework content and Meeting Invites heading; the heading-to-first-row gap remains unchanged. Right-rail order is Counseling Status, Prepare & Enrich, Next Step, Next Meeting, Progress. The rail Progress card keeps the `Session X of Y` current-position label and the existing horizontal bar calculation/presentation; this position indicator does not establish program completion. The initial Session 0 action now reads `Schedule Initial Meeting`, uses a standalone full-width rounded button, and centers its label horizontally and vertically while no Initial Meeting is scheduled; after scheduling, the existing `Schedule Next Session` split action retains its prior text alignment. Normal authored Sessions retain `Schedule Next Session`, while only a snapshot explicitly designated final shows `Complete Program`. For a normal authored Session with zero eligible published next Sessions, the progression sheet retains Homework Status, shows the explicit Next Session blocker, hides the empty selector, meeting date/time fields, and completion action, and guards submit; once a published next Session is available, the existing normal scheduling flow is unchanged. The explicit final-Session Complete Program flow remains separate. Contextual secondary actions remain wired to the existing reschedule, follow-up, individual resend, and close actions. ## Session Materials PDF Export (October 27, 2026)

- Added an on-demand Node.js PDF endpoint and shared Preview PDF / Download PDF controls for Couple Session Materials and every active/completed Session shown in an assigned Counselor's Case. The Couple control appears only on the Session Material tab; the Counselor's Session Materials list remains available for completed Cases/Sessions when the existing Case assignment still authorizes access. Preview synchronously opens a new tab to accommodate Safari popup rules; Download saves the authorized PDF with its server-provided sanitized filename without navigating the Session page. Both actions share the same route and generation implementation, prevent duplicate requests, show pending states, and use the shared safe error toast.
- The endpoint verifies the active authenticated identity, the Couple's own Case or active assigned care-team membership, and active/completed Case Session status before reading `case_session_material_blocks`. It uses the current user's Supabase client and existing RLS; it never uses service-role access. Only the requested immutable assigned snapshot is exported. It does not query current Session Builder blocks, Homework, partner profiles, or answer data. Pinned Resource versions are read under RLS; supported JPEG/PNG/WEBP/AVIF image Resources are downloaded through authenticated Storage access, size/dimension limited, and converted with Sharp. No signed URLs are included. Resource title/description use snapshot values when present and otherwise the assigned Resource's current metadata under RLS; Resource metadata itself is not versioned by the existing schema. Resource links use the authorized Resonate open/version route; external links are restricted to valid HTTP(S).
- PDFs use PDFKit in the Node runtime, US Letter portrait, a white page, official Resonate SVG mark with “Resonate Marriage Ministry” in navy alongside it, and the Session title below the branded header; they retain brand colors, continuous ordered content, text/heading/list rendering, pinned Resource descriptions/images, clickable links, a sanitized Session-title filename, inline disposition, and private no-store response headers. Unsupported content or invalid links fail the export instead of being silently omitted. No database/storage copy or migration was added. The header-only branding update does not change export content, authorization, links, page numbering, or delivery behavior.

## Phase 3D-D1 — secure Meeting notification Realtime signal (October 23, 2026)

- Added and applied DEV-only forward migration `20261023100000_meeting_notification_realtime_signal.sql`. `meeting_notification_update_signals` contains one row per Meeting (`meeting_id` primary/foreign key), its `counseling_case_id`, and an atomically incremented `bigint revision`; it contains no recipient, provider, error, dedupe, template, or resend metadata and was not backfilled. A restricted `private.advance_meeting_notification_update_signal()` trigger advances the signal for Meeting-linked delivery inserts and for actual changes to `status`, `error_category`, or `error_message`. It validates that the delivery's Meeting belongs to the supplied Case, skips intake/unlinked and unchanged updates, and never writes delivery records or invokes email/reminder behavior.
- Signal RLS grants authenticated users SELECT only. Admin/Super Admin use the established admin-role helper, while current Case staff and permitted Campus Leads use `private.current_user_can_manage_counseling_case`; anonymous, Couple, unrelated/former staff, and removed team members receive no policy. Browser roles have no signal writes. The trigger is `SECURITY DEFINER` with an empty search path, schema-qualified objects, no dynamic SQL, and no public/anonymous/authenticated execution. Existing `notification_deliveries` and `notification_webhook_events` grants and policies are unchanged.
- Added only `meeting_notification_update_signals` to `supabase_realtime`; delivery and webhook tables remain unpublished. The staff Counseling Case mounts `MeetingNotificationRealtime` only when the existing authorized view has a current Meeting. It subscribes to INSERT/UPDATE with exact `meeting_id=eq.<currentMeetingId>`, reuses the shared auth-ready bootstrap, token propagation, status/recovery handling, 150 ms coalescing, DEV isolation switch, and cleanup, and performs only `router.refresh()` against the existing authoritative `getMeetingNotificationRows` query. No passive toast, client delivery-state copy, send, retry, resend, or reminder action was added. The unused `recipient_email` was removed from the server-to-client delivery projection; server-owned sending retains it.
- Linked DEV `lctkqjjkhpyootwvttvj` migration history now aligns through `20261023100000`. Generated Supabase types were refreshed from linked DEV after excluding the shell's stale legacy management-token override; the type diff contains only the signal table. A service-role read confirmed the table exists with zero signal rows, and an anonymous read was denied with `42501`. No delivery record, fixture, Meeting, Session, Homework, email, reminder, or lifecycle state was created or changed. Independent live publication catalog inspection was unavailable without Docker/Podman, so publication membership is supported by the successfully applied atomic migration rather than a separate catalog dump.
- Focused migration/security, Realtime, shared auth-ready/isolation, Meeting Invite presentation, delivery, reminder, and Case-page tests pass. Final closeout ran 74 focused tests across eight files, `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run verify:supabase`, and `git diff --check`. The first focused run exposed that the Case-page server-render harness did not mock the newly mounted client invalidator; adding the same inert component mock used for the other Realtime invalidators corrected the test boundary without changing application behavior. The intermittent PGRST303 investigation remains separate and unresolved; the deferred Phase 3D-C2 browser limitation is unchanged.

## Phase 3D-C — Realtime Sessions & Meetings architecture/security audit (October 20, 2026)

### Existing architecture and authoritative data

- `public.case_session_meetings` is the single authoritative Meeting table for Session 0, authored-Session Meetings, and follow-up Meetings. Session 0 rows have null `session_id`/`case_session_id`; authored and follow-up rows have both. Rescheduling updates the current row in place. Follow-up scheduling and Session progression complete the current row and insert the next row. Normal product code has no Meeting cancellation/delete workflow; deletion is limited to the DEV reset utility.
- Meeting mutations stay behind the existing server actions and `SECURITY DEFINER` lifecycle RPCs in `features/counseling-sessions/actions.ts` and the lifecycle migrations. Existing actions revalidate `/workspace` and `/workspace/cases/[caseId]`, but that cache invalidation does not refresh another already-open browser. Meeting email dispatch and resend/delivery tracking remain separate after the database mutation and must not be changed by Realtime.
- Couple Workspace already queries all case Meetings and selects the newest uncompleted Initial or current authored-Session Meeting. Counselor queries project the same rows into `sessionCardState`. Existing Los Angeles date/time formatting remains server presentation logic; Realtime only requests a new authorized render.
- `public.case_sessions` is the authoritative Couple-assignment snapshot. It owns `actual_sequence`, pinned `template_sequence_number`, title/finality, release timestamps, pinned Homework revision, active/completed state, and actor/timestamps. Released Material remains in `case_session_material_blocks`; those content rows are not a Realtime signal candidate.
- Session activation/progression transactions update the old Meeting/Session and insert the next `case_sessions`/Meeting rows. Explicit final completion and incomplete closure additionally update `public.counseling_cases` (`status`, `closed_at`). Existing RPCs retain the one-active-Session invariant, Homework gate/OVERRIDE, finality, numbering, snapshots, and release rules.
- Couple Workspace, Couple Session View, and Counseling Case detail are Server Component routes backed by existing RLS-filtered server queries. Small client invalidators can mount beside their server-rendered content and call debounced `router.refresh()`; no page-sized Client Component or client copy of domain data is needed. These routes use request/session state and no application data cache that would defeat a fresh server render.

### Realtime security and publication assessment

| Candidate | Events | RLS / subscriber boundary | Payload and decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `case_session_meetings` | `INSERT`, `UPDATE` | RLS enabled; authenticated SELECT policy calls `private.current_user_can_access_case(counseling_case_id)`; subscribe with `counseling_case_id=eq.<caseId>` | Payload contains IDs, schedule/location, actor IDs, completion timestamps, and no answers/content/email. Authorized users already query it. **Publish for C1.** Exclude `DELETE`; normal lifecycle does not delete and DEV reset is not a supported live path. |
| `case_sessions` | `INSERT`, `UPDATE` | Same case-access helper; subscribe with `counseling_case_id=eq.<caseId>` | Payload contains assigned snapshot metadata and a pinned Homework revision ID, but no Material body or private answer. **Publish for C2.** Do not publish Material/Homework content tables. |
| `counseling_cases` | `UPDATE` | RLS SELECT uses the same case-access helper; subscribe with `id=eq.<caseId>` | Payload contains case/campus/Couple IDs, status, closure and audit timestamps, with no answers/content. **Publish for C2** so final completion and closure refresh even when no further Session insert occurs. |
| `case_status_history`, `case_assignments`, notification tables, Material/Homework content | none | Some rows include reasons, staffing, delivery, or private content and are not required to detect the supported state changes | **Do not publish.** Server queries remain authoritative. Notification status live updates and immediate access-revocation signaling are separate future concerns. |

- The latest `private.current_user_can_access_case` grants access only to Admin/Super Admin, campus oversight, an active Couple member with the Couple role, or an active assigned care-team member with the matching role. This protects Postgres Changes and the subsequent server refresh. Stale/ended assignments fail the helper; reconnect and every refreshed query re-evaluate authorization.
- A connected page is not an authorization authority. Event handlers must discard payload data and only trigger a server refresh. Role/assignment changes are not part of C1/C2, so immediate eviction without a Meeting/Session/Case event is deferred; do not publish `case_assignments` merely to solve that edge case. If immediate eviction becomes required, design a narrow private Broadcast/access-revocation signal separately.
- Filtered Postgres Changes is the smallest secure design because all three candidate rows are already case-scoped, RLS-protected metadata. No existing authorized change-signal table covers these mutations. Private Broadcast would reduce payload size but require triggers, `realtime.messages` authorization, and more failure modes without a present confidentiality benefit. Polling is unnecessary.
- Publication changes require forward migration(s): C1 adds `case_session_meetings`; C2 adds `case_sessions` and `counseling_cases` to `supabase_realtime`. Migration tests must prevent accidental content/notification table publication. Generated database types should not need regeneration because publication membership does not change schema.
- Read-only linked DEV migration history succeeded and matches local through `20261019100000_realtime_homework_progress.sql`. Repository migrations declare only `homework_participant_progress` in `supabase_realtime`. A fresh linked schema/catalog dump could not confirm the live publication membership because Supabase CLI required unavailable Docker/Podman; live publication membership must be confirmed before applying C1. No credential was changed.

### Phase 3D-C1 — Meeting Live Updates recommendation

1. **Signal:** filtered Postgres Changes on `case_session_meetings`, `INSERT` and `UPDATE`, exact `counseling_case_id` filter. A single transaction burst should schedule one refresh after the existing 150 ms debounce.
2. **Migration:** one forward migration adding only `public.case_session_meetings` to `supabase_realtime`, after a read-only linked catalog check confirms it is absent and the migration is the only pending DEV migration.
3. **Client integration:** mount a case-keyed Meeting invalidator on Couple Workspace and exact Counseling Case detail. Keep staff aggregate `/workspace` out of C1 to avoid an unfiltered subscription or one channel per card. Expose `caseId` through the existing Couple Workspace projection where needed.
4. **Reuse:** use the singleton browser Supabase client, Phase 3D-B subscription lifecycle, 150 ms coalescing, reconnect refresh, cleanup, and `router.refresh()`. A small feature-local helper for debounce/reconnect/cleanup is now justified by two concrete subscriptions; entity-specific table/filter registration should remain explicit. Preserve Phase 3D-B behavior and its regression tests during any extraction.
5. **Authorization:** no client writes; server/RPC authorization and table RLS remain unchanged. Never render event payloads. Meeting notification/delivery tables are excluded, so an unrelated staff tab may see new Meeting data before post-mutation email delivery history is refreshed; notification tracking Realtime is explicitly outside C1.
6. **Focused tests:** exact table/events/filter; no delete or broad `*`; debounce/concurrent-event coalescing; reconnect recovery; cleanup/key replacement; authorized Couple/staff and unrelated/ended-assignment denial; Initial, reschedule, authored, and follow-up query projections; no passive toast/email call; publication migration allowlist.
7. **Browser verification:** use separate Counselor and Couple browsers on a disposable case; schedule Session 0, reschedule it, activate an authored Session, and schedule a follow-up. Confirm current date/time/location changes without refresh, old Meetings are not presented as upcoming, timezone stays correct, no passive toast/duplicate email occurs, multiple tabs converge, and an offline mutation appears after reconnect.
8. **Complexity:** small-to-medium: one narrow migration, one case-scoped invalidator, two mount points, a small reusable lifecycle primitive, and focused RLS/browser verification.

Required C1 work is the publication migration, invalidator, mount points, projection of `caseId`, and tests. Optional is the small lifecycle-helper extraction if it can preserve the Phase 3D-B implementation exactly. Staff aggregate-list and notification-delivery live updates are deferred.

### Phase 3D-C2 — Session & Case Live Updates recommendation

1. **Signal:** extend the case-journey invalidator with `case_sessions` `INSERT`/`UPDATE` filtered by `counseling_case_id`, plus `counseling_cases` `UPDATE` filtered by `id`. Share one channel and one debounce window for these case-level signals and the C1 Meeting signals.
2. **Migration:** one later forward migration adding only `public.case_sessions` and `public.counseling_cases` to `supabase_realtime`, after the same linked-catalog/pending-migration checks.
3. **Client integration:** extend Couple Workspace and exact Counseling Case detail; also mount on the dedicated Couple Session View so program completion/closure and assigned Session state update while it is open. Return the already-authorized `counseling_case_id` as a subscription key from `getCoupleSessionViewData`. Historical list Realtime is not required after terminal completion.
4. **Reuse:** extend the C1 case invalidator rather than introduce parallel channels. Reuse all current authorized server queries, snapshot projections, numbering, released content access, and route rendering. Event payloads remain signals only.
5. **Authorization:** retain case-scoped SELECT RLS and existing RPC writes. Never publish `case_session_material_blocks`, Homework answers/progress for this milestone, closure history/reasons, staffing assignments, or notifications. A refresh after the committed Session insert can safely read its already-committed pinned Material/Homework through existing policies.
6. **Focused tests:** exact three-table/event/filter allowlist; transaction-burst coalescing; active Session replacement and one-active invariant; pinned number/finality/releases unchanged; completed history remains readable; final completion and incomplete closure refresh; unreleased/cross-case/ended-assignment denial; reconnect and cleanup; no duplicate notification/action/toast.
7. **Browser verification:** in separate Counselor/Couple tabs activate Session 1, progress to another published Session, verify released Material/Homework and pinned numbering, complete an explicit Final Session, and separately exercise incomplete closure. Confirm automatic Workspace/Session View state, history, multi-tab convergence, reconnect recovery, and authorization denial.
8. **Complexity:** medium: two publication additions, extension of the C1 invalidator, a Session View mount/projection, and lifecycle/RLS regression coverage across multi-row transactions.

Required C2 work is the two publication additions, the two explicit event registrations, three exact-page integrations, and focused tests. Optional is later coverage for an aggregate staff list. Immediate access-revocation Broadcast, notification delivery Realtime, and DEV reset synchronization are deferred enhancements.

### Fixture, regression, and implementation order

- Preserve `DEV — Realtime Homework Test` (`9e789f4d-8536-4883-97e8-23cadb9f8508`) with both partners Submitted for Phase 3D-B regression work. Do not reschedule, progress, close, or reset it for C1/C2.
- Create a separate clearly named disposable Phase 3D-C case during implementation, only after publication/auth prerequisites pass. It needs two partner accounts, an eligible care-team assignment, Session 0, at least two published authored Sessions with an explicit final Session, and safe DEV email-sink configuration. One resettable C fixture can support C1 then C2, but terminal completion/closure runs must be reset only under the approved DEV utility or use separate terminal scenarios. Do not reuse preserved Phase 3C fixtures.
- Test authorization with existing unrelated/ended-assignment identities without exposing answers or mutating their cases. Verify multi-tab bursts, offline/reconnect missed-event recovery, cleanup after navigation, and no background toasts or duplicate emails.
- Implement C1 first, validate its smaller Meeting-only surface, then extend the same case invalidator in C2. This order limits publication scope and establishes Couple/staff synchronization before multi-row progression transactions.
- Audit-only validation: no application tests/typecheck/lint were required because only this document changed. No migration, DEV row, fixture, application source, PROD resource, commit, or push was performed. Current branch is `main`; pre-existing Campus Settings edits and the mixed `PROJECT_STATE.md` work remain dirty and preserved.

October 16, 2026 — Shared toast foundation (Phase 1)

- Kept the single root-mounted Sonner provider in `app/layout.tsx`. `components/toast-provider.tsx` now centralizes bottom-right desktop placement, 24px desktop and 16px mobile offsets, system theme integration, close-button dismissal label, four-second provider fallback duration, and existing Resonate success/error/warning/info tokens. Success and error use explicit check/alert icons; the shared surface uses existing typography and rounded styling. Mobile placement uses Sonner's supported `mobileOffset` configuration.
- Feedback convention: one toast after confirmed operation success; one safe, actionable toast for operation-level failure; field validation stays inline; pending/loading stays in existing controls; avoid simultaneous inline and toast confirmation; represent partial success accurately; scheduling a Meeting does not claim invitation email delivery; routine background/realtime updates do not toast.
- Existing direct Sonner usage in Session Builder, Homework Builder, and Resource Library remains compatible. No workflow was migrated in this phase, and no application behavior, navigation, server actions, permissions, persistence, database, DEV, or PROD state changed.

Previously delivered reminders keep the same delivery key and are not resent. Supabase Cron remains exactly `meeting-reminders-hourly` at `0 * * * *`.

October 16, 2026 — Supabase Cron reminder scheduler deployment blocker
- Audited the Phase 3 reminder path. Vercel previously targeted authenticated `GET /api/cron/meeting-reminders` hourly. The route requires exact `Authorization: Bearer <CRON_SECRET>` and delegates to the existing server-only reminder processor. That processor scans only future open Meetings inside 72 hours, applies the unchanged 72-hour Homework and 24-hour Meeting rules, resolves the existing two Couple recipients, and uses the existing Resend delivery implementation and templates. Its delivery key includes Meeting ID, reminder type, current start time, and partner profile; the partial unique index on `notification_deliveries.delivery_key` is claimed before sending, so repeated or overlapping invocations cannot send the same reminder twice.
- Added forward migration `20261016100000_schedule_meeting_reminders.sql`. It enables Supabase-supported `pg_cron` and `pg_net`, refuses to schedule unless named Vault entries `meeting_reminder_endpoint_url` and `meeting_reminder_cron_secret` exist and are non-empty, removes any same-name job, and schedules one `meeting-reminders-hourly` job at `0 * * * *`. The job performs an authenticated `net.http_get` to the Vault-held endpoint. No endpoint, bearer credential, Resend secret, or project credential is embedded in source or migration.
- Removed `/api/cron/meeting-reminders` from `vercel.json`; the daily `/api/cron/keep-alive` entry remains. Commit `af9921d` deployed successfully through GitHub only to Vercel project `resonate-movement/marriage-ministry-dev`, clearing the Hobby hourly-Cron rejection. The deployed route returns 401 without authorization. A read-only DEV query found zero current reminder candidates and zero possible due 24-hour or 72-hour reminders, so an authorized probe would not send mail.
- Activation is deliberately blocked before Vault provisioning or migration apply: `.env.local` contains five ignored `CRON_SECRET` definitions, and each was tested by position without logging its value; all five received 401 from the deployed DEV endpoint. The Vercel CLI is logged out, so the deployed secret cannot be read or updated safely from this environment. Linked DEV currently has only `supabase_vault` installed, zero Vault entries, and no `cron.job` relation. Local and linked migration history align through `20261015170000`; `20261016100000` is the only pending DEV migration. There is currently no active reminder scheduler in Vercel or Supabase.
- Focused reminder/authentication validation passed 17 tests across four files. `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check` passed. The deployed DEV endpoint's unauthorized behavior was verified live. Authorized live invocation, Vault provisioning, migration apply, hourly job execution, and duplicate live invocation remain pending on the credential blocker. No lifecycle record was modified, no reminder email was sent, and PROD was not accessed.

October 12, 2026 — Phase 3C Canonical Meeting-Time State Wiring Fix
- Trace: the shared input renders `type="time"`, `name="time"`, `value={meetingTime}`, no `defaultValue`, and an `onChange` that writes `event.currentTarget.value` to `meetingTime`; it has no `onInput`. A native time input uses the normalized 24-hour value, so selecting 3:00 PM yields `15:00` even when the browser displays `03:00 PM`. Submit previously read `FormData.get("time")` instead of the controlled state, creating two value sources; the validator could therefore see an empty value while the controlled field displayed a time. The submit handler now calls `validateMeetingSchedule(meetingDate, meetingTime)` and writes the validated canonical time to the existing FormData payload. No independent display-time state was introduced.
- Inline required/invalid handling, `noValidate`, existing server-side validation and lifecycle actions remain unchanged. Because the native input remains controlled and the form closes only after a successful action, date validation failures and server action errors leave the selected time in state and rendered. The DatePicker implementation was not changed.

October 12, 2026 — Phase 3B Homework and Upcoming Meeting Reminders
- Reused the existing Vercel cron infrastructure and `CRON_SECRET` authorization by adding an hourly `/api/cron/meeting-reminders` job. The server-only processor reads the current uncompleted Meeting, case, active case Session, active Homework revision, published Homework blocks, and each partner's own progress. Initial/Session 0 Meetings receive only the 24-hour Meeting reminder; authored Session follow-ups continue to reference the same active Session and Homework assignment.
- 72-hour Homework reminders are sent independently to each partner only when the active authored Session has Homework content and that partner is not submitted/reviewed. The 24-hour Meeting reminder is sent independently to both partners regardless of Homework. Its CTA/content reflects only that recipient's Homework status; no-Homework and Session 0 messages remain Meeting-only. Templates include only Session/Meeting details, a secure Resonate URL, and the signed delivery tag; they omit answers, partner status, Prepare & Enrich, and counseling notes.
- Reminder delivery uses Phase 3A `notification_deliveries`, delivery-key uniqueness, per-event attempt history, provider IDs/status/failures, Resend suppression checks, webhook tags, and webhook status RPC. Idempotency keys include the current Meeting start time, so changing a Meeting creates a distinct future anchor while old unsent dates are no longer queried. A due reminder is skipped when Meeting `updated_at` is later than its target, preventing catch-up after schedule/reschedule inside the 72-hour or 24-hour window. No pending schedule table or migration was needed. Phase 2D cleanup already deletes case delivery rows, and webhook receipts cascade with delivery deletion; there is no extra reminder state to reset.
- Extended staff Meeting delivery history to show the 72-hour and 24-hour reminder status by partner with existing delivery history and individual resend support. Couple pages do not load delivery/provider metadata. Added the authenticated `/workspace/homework` destination for reminder CTAs, with current active published Homework content, the signed-in partner's own answers/progress, explicit save, and submit through existing partner-scoped RPCs. Submitted Homework remains read-only; a partner cannot view the other partner's state or responses.

October 13, 2026 — Phase 3A Meeting Notifications, Delivery Tracking, Resend, and Calendar
- Reused the existing Resend SDK/provider and notification delivery model. Extracted the existing server-only Supabase service-role client into `lib/supabase/service-role.ts` and reused it for intake notifications and Meeting notifications. Lifecycle actions dispatch only after their authenticated scheduling/rescheduling RPC succeeds: Session 0 schedule/reschedule, first authored Session, next authored Session, follow-up Meeting, and authored Meeting reschedule. Unique delivery keys use Meeting + event type + current start time + recipient profile to suppress duplicate automatic sends. Missing partner email is recorded as `no_email`. Resend API/provider suppression checks prevent blind sends to complained, bounced, or suppressed addresses; immediate provider and suppression-check failures remain visible to staff.
- Added a verified Resend webhook endpoint using the SDK verifier and Svix headers. Signed meeting-notification tags identify the exact delivery row, including if a webhook arrives before the send response persists its provider ID. A service-role-only RPC records event IDs with a unique constraint and transactionally updates `sent`, `delivered`, `delayed`, `failed`, `bounced`, `complained`, or `suppressed` state; duplicate/replayed event IDs do not update twice. Webhook failure details are retained after email redaction. No open/click tracking was added.

The October 13 presentation consolidation groups all notification events under that partner while retaining each event and attempt. Resend verifies case visibility and membership server-side, sends only to the selected partner using the current Meeting date/time and current Session context, creates a new attempt, and preserves prior attempts. Couple and anonymous roles have no delivery metadata policy or resend action.

Meeting emails include the current event details, a type-appropriate Couple Workspace CTA, a Google Calendar link, and an `.ics` attachment. Calendar content contains only the safe event title, date/time, and Resonate Workspace URL; authored Session email notes that Material and Homework are available, while follow-up email does not suggest a new release. October 2, 2026 — Counseling Sessions runtime error correction
- Browser review exposed `Counseling Sessions are unavailable`. Direct authenticated DEV reproduction isolated `assignmentsResult` as the failing query, with Postgres error `42P17` (infinite RLS recursion on `homework_assignment_revisions`). `caseResult`, `sessionsResult`, and `meetingsResult` were successful.
- Root cause: the Sessions care-team progress policy traversed Homework revisions while existing participant-read policies on assignments/revisions traversed revisions/progress. PostgreSQL evaluated the combined policy graph recursively for the nested `homework_assignments → homework_assignment_revisions → homework_participant_progress` select.
- Added DEV migrations `20261002110000_fix_counseling_sessions_progress_policy.sql` and `20261002120000_fix_homework_read_policy_recursion.sql`. They use narrowly scoped private Security Definer eligibility helpers and replace only the recursive policy expressions; care-team, participant, and Admin/Super Admin access semantics remain unchanged. Both migrations were applied to `lctkqjjkhpyootwvttvj`; no unrelated schema, Resource Library, Session Material, or People & Teams changes were made.
- `getCounselingSessionCards` now safely logs only query label/code/message server-side while preserving the browser-safe generic error. Focused tests now cover diagnostics and RLS migration intent. Authenticated DEV reproductions confirm all four workspace queries work for Campus Lead, and the nested Homework query works without errors for Campus Lead, Coach, and Counselor.

1. Project Overview & Goals
Product purpose
The application is an internal and participant-facing platform for Resonate Movement’s pre-engagement / marriage counseling ministry.
The system is intended to support the full counseling workflow:
- Admins receive and manage couples.
- Admins pair couples with Coaches and Counselors.
- Authors/Admins build reusable Session content and Homework.
- Counselors eventually assign relevant Session material and Homework to individual couples.
- Couples eventually consume reading/resources and complete required Homework.
- Counselors eventually review submitted responses.
- Historical assignments and participant responses must remain stable even when curriculum is revised later.
Resource Library standalone milestone and Session Material Resource Library integration are complete. Homework authoring/Preview and Session Material Video / Link behavior remain unchanged.
Runtime requirement: use Node 24 for project validation.
Core product model
A counseling curriculum consists of Sessions.
A Session is treated in the authoring UI as one document with one visible lifecycle:
- Draft
- Published
Within a Session are two main workspaces:
Session Material
Session Material is now conceptually divided into:
Pages
- Main reading/teaching material.
- Stored internally as the existing rich_text block type.
- Displayed to authors as Page, not Rich Text.
- Each Page has:
  - Title
  - Rich-text body
- Intended to become a participant-facing reading page.
- Preview displays one Page at a time.
Resources
- Supplemental material.
- Existing Video / Link remains, alongside completed Image / Document / Audio / Video library-backed integration.
- Resources are displayed separately from Pages.
- Library-backed blocks reference a stable Resource identity and resolve the current file version; DEV migration and rollback verification passed, and user browser approval is complete.
Homework
Homework is content that participants will eventually complete.
Supported authoring block types:
- Rich Text
- Video / Link
- Long Answer
Homework maintains a sophisticated independent version/history model underneath the Session UI because assignments and participant responses must remain historically correct.
The authoring UI deliberately does not expose a competing Homework Draft/Published badge. The visible lifecycle belongs to the Session as a whole.
Long-term participant model
The eventual participant experience should allow couples to:
- view assigned Session Pages;
- open supplemental Resources;
- read Homework content;
- answer Long Answer questions;
- save progress;
- submit Homework;
- retain access to appropriate Session materials.
Long Answer responses are private/individual.
Partner answer visibility has intentionally been deferred.
Future selective Session Material assignment
A major future product decision has already been made:
Counselors will eventually be able to choose which Session Pages to send to a particular couple.
Different couples may receive different subsets of the Session reading material.
Example future assignment:
Select reading pages

☑ Communication and Expectations
☑ Conflict Resolution
☐ Financial Expectations
☑ Preparing for Marriage
This feature is not implemented yet.
Important architectural constraint:
- Future assignments should reference the stable identity of a Page, not its visual position.
- Reordering Pages must not change the Page identity.
- Do not redesign the existing Page model in a way that destroys stable identity.
Resources may eventually be selected independently as well, but that has not yet been fully designed.
2. Current System Architecture / Tech Stack / Writing Style Rules
Core technology
- Next.js App Router
- React
- TypeScript
- TailwindCSS
- ShadCN UI
- Supabase
  - Authentication
  - PostgreSQL
  - RPCs
  - RLS
- Tiptap rich-text editor
- dnd-kit for accessible drag/drop
- Sonner for toast notifications
- Node 24 is the required project runtime
Use Node 24 for final validation whenever available.
Supabase environments
Marriage Ministry DEV

lctkqjjkhpyootwvttvj
Configured MCP:
supabase-marriage-ministry-dev

PROD

Do not:

- run destructive PROD operations;
- deploy to PROD;
- inspect or mutate PROD unnecessarily.

Generated Supabase types
Primary generated file:
types/database.generated.ts
Rules:
- Never hand-edit generated types just to make a task compile.
- If an RPC type is unavailable or generator nullability is wrong, isolate the accommodation in one narrow adapter.
- Do not spread casts through UI components.
Known adapter:
features/session-builder/save-session-state.ts
This currently isolates type accommodations for Session/ Homework save RPCs.
Repository engineering rules
The repository follows:
- DRY
- KISS
- YAGNI
- short readable functions
- strong reuse
- no speculative abstraction
- no dead files/exports
- no duplicated business logic when an existing implementation already owns the responsibility.
Reuse-first rule now in AGENTS.md
The repo explicitly contains the following policy:
Reuse-first rule: Before creating a new component, helper, hook, validator, editor, card shell, dialog, menu, state utility, or similar abstraction, first inspect the existing codebase for an implementation with the same responsibility. Reuse or extend the existing implementation when practical. Create a separate implementation only when there is a concrete behavioral or domain reason not to share. When a separate implementation is necessary, document the reason in the task handoff. Do not duplicate existing behavior merely to avoid a small refactor.

And:
Prefer extracting a small shared primitive over creating parallel feature-specific copies, but do not force unrelated behaviors into a single over-generalized component.

All future Codex tasks should explicitly follow this rule.
Important reuse decisions already made
These responsibilities are shared:
- RichTextEditor
- rich-text completion validation
- rich-text read-only rendering where appropriate
- rich-text collapsed summary
- VideoLinkFields
- authoring section header
- ShadCN primitives
- client HTTP(S) validation foundation
- page-level dirty/navigation protection
- exact DELETE confirmation helper
- text-field autofocus behavior in dialogs
- relevant reorder helpers
These should remain separate because the domain behavior differs:
- Session Material card wrapper
- Homework card wrapper
- Session state model
- Homework state/version model
- Session dirty comparator
- Homework dirty comparator
- Session persistence
- Homework persistence/versioning
- Session loading/error flow
- Homework loading/error flow
- unsupported Homework block handling
- assignment/history logic
Do not create a giant generic “block editor” or “content card” abstraction.
Coding-agent workflow rules
Before every Codex implementation task:
1. Discuss intended behavior with the user.
2. Wait for explicit approval.
3. Recommend a Codex model/reasoning level.
4. Then provide the Codex prompt.
Do not give an implementation prompt before approval.
Model usage
Cost-conscious defaults:
- Luna-6 Low:
  - tiny fixes
  - Git operations
  - simple presentation changes
- Luna-6 Medium:
  - normal focused UI/state implementation
  - component reuse
  - medium feature work
- Sol-6 Medium:
  - architecture
  - security
  - DB/versioning
  - substantial persistence logic
- Sol-6 High:
  - exceptional/stubborn issues only
Avoid wasting the 5-hour Codex usage window.
Validation discipline
During intermediate passes:
- run focused tests;
- run git diff --check;
- typecheck/lint once when stable;
- avoid repeatedly running:
  - full test suite
  - production build
  - database verifier.
At meaningful milestone gates:
- Node 24
- npm run typecheck
- npm run lint
- npm test
- npm run build
- git diff --check
- npm run verify:supabase when relevant
Do one full regression gate, not repeated broad loops.
Codex handoff standard
Every completed Codex task must end with:
CHATGPT HANDOFF REPORT
Only the final completion report should use that exact heading.
Reports should contain as relevant:
- branch
- files changed
- implementation behavior
- reuse decisions
- validation/tests
- DB effects
- Git status
- commit SHA if applicable
- remaining issues
- PROD confirmation.
Successful tasks should attempt a macOS say completion notification.
Sandbox restrictions sometimes prevent say; that is not considered a feature failure.
Git workflow
For completed milestones:
1. Validate.
2. Inspect complete diff/status.
3. Commit feature branch.
4. Push feature branch.
5. Merge to main.
6. Push main.
7. Delete completed feature branch locally/remotely.
8. Create next feature branch from updated main.
Do not let many completed milestones accumulate without Git synchronization.
UI / UX conventions
General
- Prefer ShadCN components.
- Avoid decorative outlines/rings unless functional.
- Avoid visual clutter.
- Mobile dialogs should use available width appropriately.
- Low-risk actions may live directly in the list/card.
- Destructive operations require deliberate confirmation.
- Keep navigation, lifecycle, and editor-state concepts visually distinct.
Breadcrumbs
Session Builder uses actual ShadCN Breadcrumb primitives.
Breadcrumbs live:
- at the top of the page;
- outside the editor card;
- above the page heading.
Existing Session:
Session Builder / Session N
New Session:
Session Builder / Create a Session
Do not reintroduce the old green SESSION N eyebrow.
Session lifecycle presentation
The Session has one visible lifecycle badge beside:
Edit Session
Draft
- warm restrained amber/gold background
- darker amber/brown text
Published
- soft green background
- darker green text
Do not display separate Draft/Published badges inside Session Material or Homework.
Unsaved state presentation
Unsaved changes is editor state, not lifecycle state.
It belongs near the Session action area.
It is driven by:
sessionDirty OR homeworkDirty
Dialog autofocus rule
App-wide dialog UX rule:
When a modal/dialog opens and contains an editable text field, the first meaningful editable text field receives focus immediately.

Examples:
- destructive DELETE confirmation
- Rich Text link dialog
- text-entry dialogs.
Dialogs without text fields retain normal Radix initial-focus behavior.
This logic is shared through:
components/ui/dialog-focus.ts
and relevant ShadCN dialog/sheet primitives.
3. Key Decisions Made So Far
Roles / permissions
Relevant roles include:
- Super Admin
- Admin
- Author
- Coach
- Counselor
- Couple
Session/Homework authoring is currently limited to:
- super_admin
- admin
- author
Authors do not gain participant answer access merely because they authored the content.
Session authoring model
A Session is edited as one staged document.
No automatic per-block persistence.
New Session
Author can:
- enter Session title;
- build Session Material locally;
- optionally Save Draft;
- directly Publish Session without saving first if valid.
Existing Draft
Author can:
- edit title/material locally;
- Save Changes;
- Publish Session including current staged changes.
Existing Published Session
Author can:
- edit locally;
- Save Changes;
- remain Published.
No requirement to create a new Session version merely because the Session itself is Published.
Session-level actions
Existing Session:
- Discard Changes
- Save Changes
Draft also has:
- Publish Session
Published Session may additionally have:
- Publish Changes
when there is a persisted pending Homework Draft.
New Session:
- Cancel
- Save Draft
- Publish Session
Discard behavior
For an existing Session:
Discard Changes restores both domains to their last persisted baseline:
- Session title/material
- Homework
It:
- makes no DB call;
- stays on the page;
- clears local dirty state.
If a Published Session already has a persisted pending Homework Draft, that Draft is the Homework baseline.
Discard must not:
- delete the saved Draft;
- revert to the previous published Homework version;
- alter assignment history.
Unsaved navigation behavior
Controlled internal navigation while dirty
Use ShadCN AlertDialog:
Discard unsaved changes?
Actions:
- Keep Editing
- Discard Changes
Browser-level exits
Use native beforeunload only for:
- refresh
- tab close
- external document navigation.
Workspace switching
Switching:
Session Material ↔ Homework
must:
- not prompt;
- not save;
- not discard;
- preserve both staged states.
Session persistence
Migration:
supabase/migrations/20260924090000_session_builder_transactional_persistence.sql
RPC:
public.save_session_builder_state(...)
Supports:
- new Draft title-only;
- new Draft with material;
- direct new Publish;
- existing Draft save;
- existing Draft publish with staged changes;
- existing Published save remaining Published.
Reconciliation:
- preserves persisted IDs;
- assigns server IDs to local blocks;
- removes omitted blocks only from current editable Session state;
- keeps contiguous order;
- rejects cross-Session IDs;
- uses row locking.
Rollback-only verifier:
features/session-builder/session-builder-transactional-dev-verification.sql
Homework Foundation
Homework Foundation is already merged and established.
Core tables:
- homeworks
- homework_versions
- homework_blocks
- homework_version_blocks
- homework_assignments
- homework_assignment_revisions
- homework_participant_progress
- homework_answers
- homework_audit_events
Assignment target:
public.counseling_cases
Homework versioning rules
One stable Homework exists per Session.
Lifecycle rules:
- Draft is editable.
- Published but never assigned remains editable.
- Once a published version has been assigned, that version is immutable.
- Editing an assigned version creates/reuses a Draft based on it.
- Publishing that Draft creates the next immutable numbered version.
Existing assignments:
- remain on their original version.
New assignments:
- use the latest published version.
Homework assignment status
Participant progress statuses:
not_started
in_progress
submitted
reviewed
Submitted answers are read-only.
Future care-role Reopen functionality is deferred.
Participant privacy
Responses are individual/private.
Partner answer visibility is deferred.
Safe initial behavior:
- each partner sees only their own answers.
Super Admin emergency Homework operations
Foundation supports:
- unassign preserving history;
- withdraw preserving history and blocking future use;
- force-update one assignment to a newer published version;
- audit events.
Force-update carry-forward
Answer carry-forward is deterministic.
An answer carries forward only when:
- same stable logical block identity;
- exact persisted prompt equality.
No AI/similarity matching.
If changed/new required work is introduced:
- previously Submitted participant may move back to in_progress.
A fully compatible explanatory-only change may preserve Submitted.
