## P1 Session Material PDF Rendering Audit (November 18, 2026)

- **Scope/status:** Audit-only branch `fix/session-material-pdf-rendering`, created from clean `main` at `75d011a7b1f2f0cc038a821104d187f9e21f93cc`. No production implementation, dependency, migration, DEV data, commit, or deployment changes. `PROJECT_STATE.md` is the only intended lasting change; a temporary synthetic PDF test harness was removed after use.
- **Architecture:** `pdfkit` 0.20.2 is the only PDF generator. Preview and Download are UI modes in `features/session-materials-export/export-session-materials-button.tsx`; both fetch `app/api/case-sessions/[caseSessionId]/materials-pdf/route.ts`, which runs the same `getAuthorizedSessionMaterials` authorization/snapshot loader and `buildSessionMaterialsPdf` generator. The only route difference is `Content-Disposition` (`inline` versus `attachment`) and the download filename handling. The renderer consumes selected immutable Session snapshot blocks ordered by position. Rich Text is stored as TipTap JSON, checked against the supported node/mark set, and traversed in `features/session-materials-export/pdf.ts`; body styling uses Helvetica, with PDFKit's wrapping plus custom cursor/page-break logic. Supported content includes paragraphs, headings, bold/italic/links, hard breaks, ordered/unordered and nested lists, blockquotes, code blocks, horizontal rules, video links, pinned resources, and supported resource images. Image processing uses `sharp`.
- **Confirmed rendering defects:** A temporary local-only PDF was generated from synthetic content (10 consecutive material blocks with long wrapping paragraphs, headings, mixed bold/italic/link marks, nested ordered/unordered lists, blockquotes, and enough content to paginate). Quick Look inspection of actual rendered pages showed (1) a section heading and the following paragraph occupying the same vertical position; (2) rich-text list bodies constrained to the marker's 24-point width, wrapping words one fragment/character at a time and overlapping neighboring list items; (3) subsequent content shifted horizontally after blockquotes; and (4) a 19-page result whose final page contained only the footer/page count. This reproduces the reported classes of overlap, list, indentation, spacing, and pagination failures without any DEV records.
- **Root causes in source:** `addText` at `features/session-materials-export/pdf.ts:41-49` emits every inline run with `continued: true`; paragraph/heading/list termination calls use an empty string with `continued: false` (`:69-83`, `:91-101`) and do not reliably advance the vertical cursor for the continued run. PDFKit's continued-text path keeps the cursor at its continuation start, matching the observed heading/paragraph overlap. List markers set `continued: true` with `width: 24` (`:91-100`), and the following inline continuation inherits that narrow layout width, directly reproducing the squeezed list text. Blockquotes assign `doc.x` at `:103-107` but do not restore the prior x position, so subsequent content inherits an indent. Pagination uses a fixed `pageBottom = 735` (`:14`) despite a 792-point Letter page with a 76-point bottom margin (usable text ends at 716 points); footer drawing begins at 735 (`:185-190`). `ensureSpace` (`:59-61`) estimates paragraphs as only 38 points and block titles as 38 points even when wrapped, while list items have no preflight. These independent estimates coexist with PDFKit's automatic text flow, making page breaks, orphan headings, and stranded/extra pages unreliable.
- **Preview/Download comparison:** The two controls share the same authorized route, snapshot loader, and generation function; the mode does not select a different layout or content pipeline. Browser inline PDF viewing versus downloaded-file viewing is a presentation difference, not an application-generated PDF difference. Repeated requests generate separate documents from the authorized Session snapshot current at each request, but use identical renderer rules. Authorization and source content are not intentionally different between modes.
- **Existing coverage and reproduction limits:** `features/session-materials-export/pdf.test.ts` checks PDF signature/page size, extracted text presence/order, links, images, and unsupported content; route tests cover auth denial, inline/attachment headers, and generic errors. These do not inspect rendered text geometry or page images. The synthetic local PDF was visually rendered with macOS Quick Look and inspected on pages 1, 2, 11, and 19. This did not use or mutate DEV data and did not reproduce a specific user-owned PDF. No production PDF was downloaded; no Vercel deployment or actual Resonate PROD was accessed.
- **Recommended implementation milestones (approval required; no fixes in this audit):** P1a, replace continuation/empty-string cursor handling with a small structured rich-text layout pass that emits style runs while explicitly advancing measured line/block height; P1b, make list markers and list-body widths independent, implement stable nested indentation/numbering, and restore x/layout state around blockquotes; P1c, base content/footer bounds and page-break checks on actual page geometry/margins, reserve heading-plus-following-content space, and avoid mixing manual and automatic pagination without a single owner; P1d, keep Preview/Download on this single renderer and add synthetic regression fixtures. PDFKit appears suitable for this scope; the defect is the custom renderer's layout bookkeeping, not a demonstrated library limitation, so a second PDF library is not recommended absent findings during implementation.
- **Proposed regression tests:** Add generated multi-page fixtures covering long/short paragraphs, headings after near-bottom content, mixed inline marks/links, nested list widths and marker alignment, quote-to-following-paragraph indentation, consecutive blocks, images/resources, and page-boundary cases. Assert extracted text/order plus measurable non-overlapping text bounding boxes and content bounds above the footer on every page; rasterize representative pages for human review. Assert Preview and Download call/receive the same PDF bytes and differ only in disposition/filename behavior. Keep authorization route tests intact.
- **Git/next steps:** All eight stash object IDs listed in the A6 closeout section above were rechecked and remain untouched. No stash was restored or dropped. Current branch is `fix/session-material-pdf-rendering`; no implementation changes, commit, push, or deployment occurred. Next step: review this audit and approve a focused PDFKit renderer correction with the proposed regression suite.

## N2 Homework Completion Notification Architecture Audit (November 18, 2026)

- **Scope/status:** Audit-only continuation on `feature/homework-completion-notification`, created from the clean `main`/`origin/main` baseline `25bc4f3693aa8950eaa40ee94b6e10da77f0952e`. No application code, database, email, environment configuration, or deployment was changed. No email was sent. The branch is intentionally uncommitted pending review of this audit and approval of an implementation strategy.
- **Homework write and review paths:** Couple drafts/submissions enter through `features/couple-workspace/homework-participant-actions.ts` and the authenticated `persist_participant_homework` RPC. Its current definition is successively replaced through the assignment-level, selection-question, and Side-by-Side migrations; the current version in `20261116110000_homework_side_by_side_responses.sql` validates the pinned revision and all required answer types before setting one `homework_participant_progress` row to `submitted` with `submitted_at`. The RPC serializes participants on the shared active `homework_assignments` row and locks the submitting progress row. Reopening enters through `features/counseling-sessions/homework-reopen-actions.ts` and `reopen_participant_homework`; the RPC locks the Case, active `case_sessions` row, assignment, revision, and target progress, then changes only that participant to `in_progress`, clears `submitted_at`, and writes an append-only `homework_reopened` audit event and private in-app notification. Current progress is stored in `homework_participant_progress` (one row per profile/revision); answers are in `homework_answers` (one row per progress/block), while Session identity and the pinned Homework revision are in `case_sessions`. The current progress timestamp is overwritten at a later submission; no append-only submission-cycle event exists. Therefore cycle history cannot safely be reconstructed from the two mutable progress rows alone. Existing audit events capture reopen/authoring events, not each submission transition.
- **Eligibility and review authorization:** Existing Counselor-of-record scope is `case_assignments.assignment_type = 'counselor'` with `ended_at IS NULL`, an active assigned group, active group membership, and role/group-type alignment (`counselor_team`/Counselor, `coach_team`/Coach, `campus_lead_team`/Campus Lead). `private.current_user_can_manage_counseling_case` is the shared case-level gate; the Homework review RPC additionally requires submitted/reviewed progress, excludes Couple members, and returns only the selected participant's answers. The route is `app/workspace/cases/[caseId]/homework/[progressId]/page.tsx`; it requires an active counseling workspace, checks the requested case, and loads the authorized record via `get_counselor_submitted_homework_review` through `features/counseling-sessions/queries.ts`. The current route is one-participant-at-a-time, not a consolidated Session review page. No new access to answers is needed for the notification itself; the email should contain no answer payload.
- **Email and delivery infrastructure:** Resend is wrapped by the server-only `features/notifications/provider.ts` using existing `RESEND_API_KEY`, `NOTIFICATION_FROM_EMAIL`, and `NOTIFICATION_REPLY_TO_EMAIL`. `features/notifications/templates.ts` contains generic intake mail; the existing branded Resonate logo/inline email example is `features/notifications/onboarding-reminder-template.ts`, and meeting mail is in `features/notifications/meeting-template.ts`. There is no existing Homework-completion email. `notification_deliveries` is the shared persistent delivery history; it has event/entity/template/recipient/status/provider identifiers, attempt numbers and a unique nullable `delivery_key`. Meeting dispatch inserts that row before sending and uses a unique delivery key to suppress duplicate request creation, but sends synchronously and has no durable retry worker or lease protocol. A signed Resend webhook route exists at `app/api/webhooks/resend/route.ts`; its current persistence RPC tracks delivery events associated with existing meeting notification IDs. The separate `onboarding_reminder_deliveries` queue has unique event identity, claim tokens, lease/claim logic, eligibility recheck, and service-role-only RPCs; its hourly processor intentionally marks uncertain provider outcomes as uncertain instead of retrying them. It is a domain-specific queue and does not implement the approved four-step Homework retry schedule. Existing scheduling uses Supabase `pg_cron`/`pg_net`, Vault-held endpoint and `CRON_SECRET`; meeting and onboarding jobs run hourly. Vercel `vercel.json` retains only the daily keep-alive job. No Homework completion schedule exists.
- **DEV mail safety:** The onboarding reminder processor has a strict dedicated-DEV identity check and exact Mailinator recipient allowlist. Meeting notification code has a local-development sink switch, but that alone is not an explicit production-like dedicated-DEV recipient allowlist. The audit did not inspect or print any environment values, query application data, invoke an endpoint, or deliver mail. Any implementation/controlled DEV send must fail closed unless the dedicated Vercel DEV identity, Supabase DEV ref, explicit synthetic-Mailinator recipient allowlist, and opt-in are positively verified; do not reuse a weaker generic meeting path as its safety boundary.
- **Recommended minimal architecture (approval required):** Extend the existing `notification_deliveries` history with a narrowly scoped Homework-completion outbox contract rather than sending from the Couple action or creating a parallel notification service. Add a forward migration for the exact Session/cycle identity, due time, lease/claim token, and cancellation/reconciliation fields and a unique `(case_session_id, completion_cycle)` constraint (or equivalent stable cycle UUID). In the same transaction as participant submission, after the existing required-answer validation and status transition, use the shared assignment lock to serialize the two partner writes, read the two authorized active Couple progress rows for the Session's pinned revision, and insert one cycle/outbox row only on the transition that makes both submitted. A unique database constraint is the final exactly-once-record guard. A submission that remains draft, a first partner submission, or a duplicate/unchanged submitted request creates no cycle. Reopening marks any undelivered cycle cancelled under the same assignment serialization boundary; a later second transition back to Submitted creates the next monotonic cycle and retains the old row. Do not hold a database transaction open over Resend calls.
- **Worker, retries, and recipient selection:** Use a small protected server-only cron endpoint and a service-role claim/finalize RPC. Claim due records in a short transaction with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, a per-row lease/claim token, bounded batch size, and stale-lease handling; only the current claim owner may finalize. A worker should re-check that the same cycle is still current, both participants remain Submitted for the same pinned revision, the Case/Session remains eligible, and the recipient is an active member of the currently assigned active Counselor-of-record team immediately before sending. If Homework was reopened before claim, cancel rather than send stale “ready” mail. If assignment changes while queued, never send to the former Counselor; recommended behavior is to resolve the then-current eligible Counselor on the existing cycle before send (no new cycle/email event from reassignment alone), otherwise cancel with a durable reason. The precise treatment of reassignment and already-claimed in-flight sends needs approval because database state and an external provider call cannot be committed atomically. Keep delivery failures invisible to Couples.
- **Retry/idempotency limits:** Persist the initial attempt and each attempt outcome, with due times for retries after 5 minutes, 15 minutes, 1 hour, and 6 hours; terminally record permanent rejection, invalid/suppressed recipient, or exhausted attempts. A minute-level DEV Supabase Cron poll can make initial delivery prompt and process due backoff times with at most roughly one poll interval of scheduling delay. Use one stable key derived from the durable completion-cycle identity and byte-for-byte-equivalent payload on every provider retry. Resend documents email idempotency support for 24 hours and rejects reuse of a key with a different payload; this materially reduces duplicate sends during the proposed retry window, but is not indefinite exactly-once delivery. A timeout/worker loss after provider acceptance is ambiguous. Preserve `uncertain` state and reconcile by provider message/webhook evidence; do not blindly send again after the provider's idempotency window expires. The existing signed Resend webhook must be extended narrowly to correlate Homework completion deliveries, or the provider status must otherwise be safely reconciled. Exact permanent/transient error classification, handling of ambiguous outcomes, and exhausted-attempt user/admin visibility require approval. Source: [Resend idempotency key behavior and 24-hour retention](https://resend.com/changelog/idempotency-keys).
- **Email content and link decision:** Render the approved initial/subsequent subject prefixes with the two ordered Couple first names; render full names in male-first display order, Session number/title and submitted status, and a direct authenticated DEV/app URL. Reuse the Resonate logo and email-compatible styles from the existing branded reminder template; escape all dynamic text/URL values. Do not include answers, scores, or private counseling notes. The existing review route takes one `progressId`; it does not display both participants' submitted answers together. Before implementation, approve whether the notification should link to the current Case detail (which exposes both authorized participant review links) or whether a small Session-level review route is required. Do not select a single partner's answer page as the notification destination without approval.
- **Migration/configuration likely required:** No existing migration can safely deliver these semantics. Expect a forward-only migration for durable completion-cycle/outbox identity, delivery scheduling/lease/cancellation fields or equivalent tables, indexes/uniqueness, tightly granted service-role claim/finalize and role-authorized enqueue boundary, plus webhook correlation as needed. Do not alter existing migrations or broaden RLS. Reuse `RESEND_API_KEY`, `NOTIFICATION_FROM_EMAIL`, `NOTIFICATION_REPLY_TO_EMAIL`, `APP_URL`, `CRON_SECRET`, and the existing Vault/`pg_net` pattern where validated. Plan a narrowly scoped new queue endpoint/job and Vault endpoint entry with a minute-level schedule; do not modify existing meeting/onboarding jobs. Add an explicit dedicated-DEV-only opt-in and Mailinator recipient allowlist (reuse an existing exact safety abstraction only if it can safely express Counselor recipients and the verified DEV project). Verify variable names/config presence without disclosing values. No migrations, environment changes, scheduler changes, or emails were performed in this audit.
- **Verification plan:** Unit tests for subject/body escaping, exact status-only content, partner ordering, CTA construction, classification/backoff, stable idempotency key, eligibility changes, and terminal/uncertain states. Application integration tests for draft/first submission/duplicate submit/no Counselor, assignment-at-completion, reopen-before-claim, reassignment-before-claim, failure not blocking submission, and subsequent completion cycles. Self-contained rollback-only linked-DEV SQL tests should create isolated Couple, Case, active Counselor group/member/assignment, Session, published Homework revision, partner progress and answers in one transaction; exercise actual RPCs under simulated authenticated roles; assert one durable cycle, rollback and zero fixtures. Add genuine simultaneous two-session submissions and concurrent worker claims against isolated DEV fixtures to prove no missed/duplicate event and single claim ownership; role simulation is not a substitute for real concurrent sessions. Exercise transient retry schedule, permanent error, provider timeout/ambiguous outcome, duplicate Resend key, and webhook replay using a controlled synthetic Mailinator DEV address only after explicit approval and DEV safety verification. Validate no answer text enters email/logs, assigned current recipient only, former/unassigned Counselor denial, Couple email privacy, and no fixture leftovers. No such test or email was run during this audit.
- **Approval decisions before implementation:** (1) confirm cancellation of a queued notification when Homework is reopened before delivery; (2) confirm whether reassignment while queued retargets the same cycle to the new current Counselor or cancels it; (3) approve the existing Case-detail link versus a new consolidated Session review route; (4) define whether “retries after” means four retries after the initial attempt and the terminal status after the 6-hour retry; (5) approve how ambiguous delivery is reconciled and whether delivery/bounce webhooks change retry/terminal states; (6) approve a dedicated DEV Mailinator allowlist and whether/how the relevant notification actor may inspect delivery history without exposing it to Couples; (7) confirm that missing/non-sendable Counselor email records a terminal no-recipient cycle and does not send later from an assignment-only change.
- **Git/status:** Branch is `feature/homework-completion-notification` at baseline `25bc4f3693aa8950eaa40ee94b6e10da77f0952e` plus this uncommitted documentation update. The eight stash object IDs were preserved unchanged: `684f8f76c4cd019854bf83bca8e928bfe9646532`, `88a2440fad42752f76d507aba3c03ba2c56f892a`, `441cff42446e3efb2cfea35d163f603a9b66b990`, `e3b498f81cb644ea6b5d78b15d9df74c3b06231b`, `b7c3cda01e73ebc0a88835b9bfab6bde67a1d71d`, `16dd8164ed400426bbab7ef330c66448b564faad`, `6ca55c504d83b82de5a0ab88f444279f21a14aa3`, and `697b7f009140f90e54b968ebb4a9d957641cb434`. No AWHS files or changes, commits, pushes, migrations, database queries/writes, deployments, or emails were made.
- **Next step:** Review and approve the listed lifecycle, reassignment, route, retry, failure, and DEV mail-safety decisions. Only after approval should implementation begin on this branch with a forward migration and self-contained concurrency/authorization verification.

## N4A Homework Completion Notification Database Foundation (November 18, 2026)

- **Status/scope:** Implemented locally on `feature/homework-completion-notification`, based on `25bc4f3693aa8950eaa40ee94b6e10da77f0952e`. This is a database-foundation-only review candidate. The forward migration has **not** been applied to DEV; no email, worker, provider webhook, Admin UI, real record change, commit, push, merge, or deployment occurred. The prior N2 architecture audit above remains historical context; this N4A work implements the approved decisions in this task and does not retroactively change N2's audit record.
- **Cycle/outbox model:** `20261118100000_homework_completion_notification_foundation.sql` adds `homework_completion_notifications`, uniquely keyed by `(case_session_id, completion_cycle)` and a stable provider idempotency key derived from the immutable notification ID. The row preserves the Session, Case, pinned revision, both participant progress IDs, completion time, Counselor-of-record assignment/team, recipient profile IDs, attempt/retry/lease fields, provider message ID, terminal status, and the single manual-recovery audit fields. `homework_completion_notification_events` records append-only status history, including actor, attempt, provider event identity, and safe detail codes. No historical completion cycles are backfilled.
- **Atomic detection and existing workflow reuse:** The public participant persistence RPC is replaced by a wrapper that first locks the applicable `case_sessions` row, then the existing assignment/progress rows, delegates unchanged paired-response and answer validation to the existing authorized persistence function, and enqueues only on a successful transition into `submitted`. Enqueueing requires exactly two active Couple progress rows for the Session's pinned active revision and both in `submitted`/`reviewed`. The Session row lock serializes partner submissions and cycle-number allocation. The existing `reopen_participant_homework` RPC remains the authorization and audit/in-app-notification implementation; its wrapper adds cancellation of `pending`/`claimed` outbox work for that cycle. Reopen preserves the previous immutable cycle row; the next complete pair of submissions receives the next cycle number. No direct invitation, notification email, or answer content is created by this migration.
- **Recipient eligibility and state behavior:** At completion, the outbox snapshots the single active `assignment_type='counselor'` Counselor-of-record group and its two active, role-aligned profiles. Missing or invalid eligibility creates a durable `blocked` cycle rather than sending or silently dropping it. Assignment, group, membership, profile role/status, Homework unassignment, and revision-ending changes cancel queued/claimed cycles when eligibility is lost. Before future delivery, a service-role-only authorization RPC rechecks assignment/group/member eligibility, the pinned active Homework revision, and both submitted progress rows. States include pending, claimed, sent, delivered, failed, blocked, skipped, cancelled, and uncertain; no plaintext error, answer, or email is persisted in the event details.
- **Future delivery support (database contracts only):** Service-role-only RPCs provide `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` leased claims, pre-delivery eligibility revalidation, claim-token-protected completion, the approved 5-minute / 15-minute / 1-hour / 6-hour retry schedule, terminal failure, and `uncertain` status on expired worker leases to avoid unsafe automatic redelivery. Admin/Super Admin may request one serialized, audited recovery attempt for eligible `failed`/`uncertain` rows; Couples cannot. The same provider idempotency key is retained across attempts. No cron schedule, edge/function worker, template, webhook handler, provider request, environment variable, or UI is created here. An external provider call cannot be transactionally atomic with Counselor reassignment; the future worker must make its final eligibility RPC immediately before sending, but a narrow in-flight send/reassignment race cannot be eliminated once a provider request has begun. This limitation needs to remain explicit in N-01 release verification.
- **Security and compatibility:** Outbox/event tables have RLS, no direct write grants (including service role), and Admin/Super Admin read-only policy. RPCs use `SECURITY DEFINER`, empty search paths, and narrow grants: participant persistence/reopen remain authenticated-only; claim, delivery authorization, and attempt completion are service-role-only; manual recovery is authenticated Admin/Super Admin-only. Existing Session assignment/progress locks, answer validation, reopening audit, participant isolation, and case authorization are delegated to existing RPCs rather than rewritten. No RLS policy is broadened and no existing migration was modified.
- **Tests and limitations:** Added a focused migration contract suite and extended `supabase/tests/homework_paired_response_runtime.sql` with rollback-only assertions for first-partner no-enqueue, second-partner single cycle, duplicate submit rejection, stable idempotency/recipient snapshot, Couple outbox invisibility, authenticated worker/manual-recovery denial, Admin read and invalid recovery state, reopen cancellation, resubmission cycle preservation, and recipient-loss cancellation. The focused suite passed **9/9** tests across the new migration contract and existing Homework reopen action coverage; `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, and `git diff --check` passed. The SQL runtime verifier was not run: this migration is intentionally unapplied and local Supabase requires Docker/Podman, which is unavailable. `supabase status` confirmed the linked project reference is the expected DEV `lctkqjjkhpyootwvttvj`; it did not run migrations, query project data, or connect to PROD. SQL execution, actual row-lock/concurrent-submit behavior, DB lint, privilege simulation, and rollback/fixture cleanup therefore remain **unverified** until a separately approved DEV migration verification window. No fixtures were created.
- **Deferred work:** N-01 worker/provider integration, branded email content, controlled DEV recipient allowlisting, cron/retry execution, provider delivery callbacks, operator UI/history, real simultaneous-submit and multi-worker tests, and controlled delivery verification remain separate follow-up work. N-02 and N-03 are retained as deferred notification milestones; their detailed scopes are not specified in this N4A request and must be defined before implementation rather than inferred. Existing unrelated deferred issues remain unchanged, including C-04/C-05, UI-02/UI-05, RT-01, T-01–T-05, and the rest of the issue register above.
- **Git/stashes/next step:** Worktree changes are limited to the N4A migration, the focused migration contract test, the rollback-only paired-response SQL verifier, and this state update. HEAD remains the original feature base, with no commit/push/merge/deploy. All eight original stash objects remain unchanged. Next: review the local database foundation and SQL verifier; after approval, apply only this migration to verified DEV and run the rollback-only SQL/database-lint/authorization/concurrency checks before beginning any worker or email implementation.

## N4A.2 Homework Completion Notification Pre-Migration Review (November 18, 2026)

- **Review result: NOT READY TO APPLY.** Static review of `20261118100000_homework_completion_notification_foundation.sql`, its contract tests, and `supabase/tests/homework_paired_response_runtime.sql` found two required safety corrections and one unresolved in-flight delivery/history gap. No migration was applied, no linked SQL was run, no DEV data was read or changed, and no email/provider action occurred. The prior N4A verification text mentioning Docker applies only to local Supabase; linked DEV SQL verification can use the repository's documented `supabase db query --linked --project-ref <DEV_REF> --file <path>` workflow and does not require Docker. That linked command was not run during this review.
- **Verified by source review — existing Homework contract:** The migration keeps the externally called `persist_participant_homework(uuid,jsonb,text)` and `reopen_participant_homework(uuid,uuid)` signatures/return types, delegates to their existing paired-response validation and authorized reopen implementations, and restores `authenticated` execution on the public RPCs while revoking direct execution from `public`/`anon`. Application call sites use those public RPC names. The migration does not backfill historical submitted records; cycle creation is only hooked to a successful transition into `submitted`. This is a static compatibility review; deployed catalog dependencies, effective grants, runtime validation, and real lock behavior remain unverified until the migration is separately approved and applied to DEV.
- **Verified by source review — cycle serialization and idempotency:** The submit wrapper locks the Session row before the existing assignment/progress locks; cycle numbering is derived while that Session lock is held, and `(case_session_id, completion_cycle)` plus unique immutable notification/idempotency identity provide database duplicate guards. The outbox snapshots the pinned revision, progress IDs, current Counselor-of-record assignment/team, and role-aligned recipient IDs. Drafts, first-partner completion, unchanged/duplicate submissions, and existing historical submissions do not enqueue. Reopen cancels pending/claimed work and preserves the old cycle row; later submission can allocate the next cycle. Assignment, team, membership, role/status, unassignment, and revision-end triggers cancel work when eligibility is lost. Missing/ineligible Counselor state creates a durable `blocked` record. The design does not retarget a queued event on reassignment; completion after reassignment snapshots the then-current eligible assignment, and assignment changes alone do not enqueue a replacement.
- **Required correction 1 — uncertain recovery is prohibited by the approved requirement:** `request_homework_completion_notification_recovery` currently accepts both `failed` and `uncertain` rows (migration around lines 788–821). The user requirement says uncertain or accepted deliveries cannot be manually retried. `sent`/`delivered` are excluded, but `uncertain` is not. Before apply, change the eligibility predicate so an uncertain/possibly accepted provider request cannot be resent; if later provider evidence is used to resolve uncertainty, that must be a separate non-send reconciliation transition. Add positive/negative tests proving only eligible, definitely failed records can consume the single audited recovery action.
- **Required correction 2 — stale claim finalization:** `finish_homework_completion_notification_attempt` checks `status='claimed'` and claim token, but does not require `lease_expires_at > clock_timestamp()` (around lines 740–743). Until the expiry sweep runs, a worker can finalize after its lease expired. Require a currently valid lease when finalizing, and test expired-token rejection both before and after another worker's claim. Keep stale workers unable to overwrite a newer outcome.
- **Required correction 3 — cancellation can erase an in-flight provider outcome:** Reopen and eligibility triggers turn both `pending` and `claimed` rows into `cancelled` and clear their claim token/lease. If a worker passed the authorization RPC and the provider accepted the request immediately before reopen/reassignment, its later `finish` call will return no row because the status/token no longer matches; the durable row then says `cancelled`, not sent/accepted/uncertain. This does not satisfy accurate in-flight outcome recording. Before apply, preserve a cancellation-requested/in-flight state or equivalent attempt-level immutable outcome record, and define a safe late-outcome reconciliation path that does not permit a stale worker to change current send eligibility or requeue an accepted message. A final DB eligibility check cannot make an external provider call atomic with reassignment/reopen; the narrow race must remain documented and be resolved in the state model rather than hidden as cancellation.
- **Other reviewed controls:** Table RLS is enabled; authenticated reads are policy-limited to Admin/Super Admin; direct table write grants are revoked including service role. Private helpers and public RPCs use `SECURITY DEFINER` with empty `search_path`; worker claim/authorize/finish are granted only to `service_role`, while manual recovery is authenticated and role-checked for Admin/Super Admin. `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, claim tokens, a 15-minute lease, one stable provider key, and automatic retry scheduling at 5 minutes, 15 minutes, 1 hour, and 6 hours are present. Attempt count yields five total automatic attempts (initial plus four retries), then `failed`; permanent outcomes fail, `skipped` is terminal, no-eligible-recipient is `blocked`, and reopen/eligibility loss are `cancelled`. A manual recovery request is serialized and audited, but it currently marks the recovery as used when queued rather than when an actual provider attempt is claimed; define whether a canceled-before-claim request consumes the one recovery allowance and make the audit semantics match. Provider-accepted `sent`/`delivered` states are not manually retryable. No worker, webhook, provider delivery, or UI exists in this migration, so runtime delivery and provider callback behavior are not claimed.
- **Tests and gaps:** The migration contract test is source-string based and currently passes its prior assertions without proving SQL behavior; it does not test the uncertain recovery defect, stale lease finalization, exact effective grants, retry transitions, late outcomes, or idempotency against changed payloads. The paired-response runtime SQL file is self-contained for synthetic Auth/Couple/Case/Counselor/Session/Homework records inside `BEGIN … ROLLBACK`, but notification-specific assertions cover only enqueue/no-enqueue, reopen/resubmission, recipient-loss cancellation, Couple visibility, and a pending-recovery denial. It does not yet exercise retry states, five-attempt limit, failed/uncertain/sent/delivered recovery denial, expired/stale claims, concurrent workers, or external-provider outcomes. No tests were run in this review; earlier N4A focused-test/typecheck/lint results remain as previously recorded and do not validate these findings.
- **Prepared DEV verification plan (not executed):** After correction approval and migration application to positively verified DEV `lctkqjjkhpyootwvttvj`, run the self-contained rollback SQL through the existing linked `supabase db query --linked --project-ref lctkqjjkhpyootwvttvj --file <sql-file>` method. In one `BEGIN`/`ROLLBACK` verifier, create uniquely tagged synthetic Auth profiles, Couple and Counselor teams, case assignment, Session, published revision, and paired progress; suppress any external dispatcher (none is included in this migration); set local authenticated claims/roles; call the actual public submission/reopen RPCs; assert first submit/draft/duplicate do not enqueue, second submit creates exactly one cycle, reopen cancels, resubmission creates cycle 2, reassignment/member/profile loss blocks or cancels, and unauthorized role/RLS boundaries hold. Under service-role simulation, assert claim exclusivity, valid-vs-expired claim finalization, each scheduled retry and terminal fifth attempt, permanent/skipped/blocked/cancelled states, immutable cycle/payload/idempotency identity, and allowed/denied manual recovery. Confirm transaction ends in `ROLLBACK`, then perform a read-only synthetic-tag residue check. Run linked migration dry-run and DB lint only after an approved apply; neither was run here.
- **Genuine concurrency plan:** A single rollback transaction and simulated role claims cannot prove inter-session lock behavior. For two simultaneous partner submissions and two worker claims, use a separate, isolated synthetic fixture provisioned in a committed setup transaction (unique test tag, no real Couple/Case), then two independent linked DEV database sessions with barriers to overlap the actual RPC calls. Assert one cycle and one claim owner, capture only fixture IDs, and delete only those tagged synthetic rows in an explicit cleanup transaction; verify zero remaining tagged rows. This creates temporary committed DEV fixtures and is a distinct, approval-required test because it cannot be rolled back atomically with both concurrent sessions. It is not a reason to require Docker.
- **Compatibility and rollback risk:** This is a forward migration that renames/moves existing RPC implementations into `private` and installs wrappers, adds triggers, and creates durable history tables. Transactional migration application can roll back if the migration itself errors, but after a successful commit an ad hoc drop would remove cycle/audit history and would need to restore the exact prior RPC bodies/grants. Prepare and review a forward-safe correction/backout plan before application; do not edit previously applied migrations or drop notification history. No database catalog dependency, migration dry-run, lint, SQL execution, or fixture-cleanup result is claimed in this review.
- **Git and next step:** Branch remains `feature/homework-completion-notification` at `25bc4f3693aa8950eaa40ee94b6e10da77f0952e`; existing N4A changes remain uncommitted. This review updated only `PROJECT_STATE.md`. The four pre-existing N4A paths remain: modified `PROJECT_STATE.md`, modified `supabase/tests/homework_paired_response_runtime.sql`, untracked `features/counseling-sessions/homework-completion-notification-migration.test.ts`, and untracked `supabase/migrations/20261118100000_homework_completion_notification_foundation.sql`. ## N4A.3 Homework Notification Database Safeguards (November 18, 2026)

- **Status:** Applied the approved safeguards to the still-unapplied `20261118100000_homework_completion_notification_foundation.sql`; it has not been applied to local or linked DEV. No worker, email, webhook, template, UI, database deployment, or real-record change was made. This remains a pre-migration review candidate; runtime behavior cannot be claimed until the migration is separately reviewed and applied to verified DEV.
- **N4A-01 recovery eligibility:** Admin/Super Admin recovery now requires a failed parent cycle with at least one durably started attempt, and every started attempt must be explicitly `rejected_retryable` or `rejected_permanent`. Uncertain, accepted, delivered, bounced, skipped, canceled, and otherwise ineligible histories are denied. Recovery reserves once under the locked parent row, derives the corrected payload from the currently eligible Counselor-of-record instead of caller-supplied IDs, and records a distinct manual-recovery idempotency key. Provider uncertainty reconciliation is a separate service-role-only, outcome-only RPC; it requires provider evidence and never enqueues or sends.
- **N4A-02 leases:** Claims retain a unique claim token, lease expiry, and monotonically increasing claim sequence. Provider start and normal finalization require the same current token and unexpired parent and attempt leases. Expired unstarted claims are recorded as `expired_before_start`, then may be reclaimed with a new token without incrementing provider-attempt count. Expired started attempts become `uncertain` and cannot be automatically resent; stale finalization returns no result. `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` and parent-first lock ordering serialize claim/finalization transitions.
- **N4A-03 cancellation:** Reopen and eligibility-loss transitions cancel pending/claimed cycles, clear their lease/schedule, and mark unstarted claim rows `cancelled_before_start`. Already-started attempts remain separately recorded, with `cancellation_requested_at`; their eventual provider evidence can be recorded by the restricted reconciliation RPC. A late accepted/delivered outcome updates immutable attempt history and the parent outcome only when the parent was not canceled; canceled parents remain terminal and are never returned to sendable state. Reconciliation reads the attempt's parent ID, then locks parent before attempt, matching the other transition lock order. Recipient-loss cancellation also matches corrected manual-recovery group/profile snapshots, not only the original snapshot.
- **N4A-04 recovery accounting:** Reservation records actor/time and is one-time under a `FOR UPDATE` parent lock plus the single-reservation guard and unique idempotency-key index. Reservation/queueing/claiming do not consume the attempt. The attempt is consumed atomically only at the durable `started` boundary immediately before the future worker may call the provider; actor/time are retained and immutable. Canceling a reserved-but-not-started recovery is terminal, clears pending state, records `cancelled_before_start`, and leaves the attempt unused. A consumed recovery cannot be requested again. This identifies the durable application/provider boundary; it cannot make an external network call transactional.
- **Regression coverage:** Added source-contract assertions for confirmed rejection eligibility, reservation locking and uniqueness, durable-start accounting, valid/unexpired lease enforcement on start/finalization, expired-start versus expired-prestart behavior, canceled outcome reconciliation, role grants, and corrected-recipient cancellation matching. Extended `supabase/tests/homework_paired_response_runtime.sql` with rollback-only synthetic role-simulation cases for expired lease recovery, stale worker denial, late accepted outcome reconciliation, recovery denial for uncertain/accepted/delivered/canceled/bounced histories, one reservation, cancellation before provider start, corrected recipient snapshot, and attempt consumption/history after provider start. Service-role simulation invokes only the narrow RPCs; direct table-state assertions run after `RESET ROLE`, respecting the intentionally revoked direct service-role table grants. The verifier remains one `BEGIN … ROLLBACK` transaction and sends no email.
- **Concurrency scope/limitation:** The contracts now explicitly retain the serialized row lock for recovery reservation, `SKIP LOCKED` worker claims, unique cycle/claim sequence/idempotency constraints, and parent-first transition locks. The rollback verifier exercises duplicate reservation and expired-claim recovery sequentially, not genuinely simultaneous independent database sessions. True two-session submission/worker races require a separately approved committed synthetic fixture plus explicit cleanup; no persistent fixture or concurrency session was created in this task. These runtime and true-concurrency checks remain required before migration application/release.
- **Validation and migration readiness:** Focused migration/action tests passed **14/14 across 2 files**. The first run exposed one mistaken test assertion (it searched for the migration-level unique index inside the function slice); that assertion was corrected before the passing rerun. `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, and `git diff --check` passed. The rollback-only DEV SQL verifier was not run because the migration is intentionally unapplied; applying it is explicitly out of scope. No database lint, DEV migration alignment, or rollback/fixture-cleanup claim is made for this unapplied schema. The migration must receive another pre-migration review before any application.
- **Files and Git:** N4A.3 changes are limited to the existing unapplied migration, the focused migration contract test, the rollback-only SQL verifier, and this document. Branch remains `feature/homework-completion-notification`; no commit, push, merge, deployment, stash operation, AWHS change, or PROD access occurred. All eight existing stash objects remain unchanged.

Resolve any further findings and obtain explicit approval before applying the migration to DEV; only then run the linked rollback verifier, DB lint/history checks, and separately authorized two-session concurrency validation.

## N4A.4 Homework Notification Final Pre-Migration Security Review (November 19, 2026)

- **Overall review: NOT READY TO APPLY.** Independent source review covered the current unapplied migration, RPCs, grants/RLS, and extended SQL verifier. N4A-01–04 are substantially corrected in code, but important gaps remain around provider payload idempotency, reconciliation evidence idempotency, and runtime/concurrency verification. No migration was applied, no DEV database query was made, no email or real data was touched, and no PROD access occurred.
- **N4A-01 recovery eligibility — source review PASS:** `request_homework_completion_notification_recovery` locks the notification row, requires parent status `failed`, requires at least one started attempt, rejects any started attempt not explicitly rejected as retryable/permanent, and denies an existing reservation/use. Thus uncertain, accepted, sent, delivered, bounced, skipped, canceled, and mixed histories are not eligible. Corrected recipient IDs are derived from the active Counselor-of-record and saved under the one-time recovery reservation; caller-supplied recipient values are not accepted. Recovery reservation contention is serialized by the row lock, with a unique recovery idempotency-key index as an additional guard.
- **N4A-02 leases — source review PASS, runtime UNVERIFIED:** Provider authorization and normal finalization verify both the parent claim token/state/lease and attempt token/state/lease. A definitely pre-start expired claim becomes `expired_before_start` and can be reclaimed using a fresh token without incrementing provider-attempt count. A started expiry becomes `uncertain` and cannot automatically return to pending. Parent-first locks are used by claim, start, finish, cancellation, and reconciliation; `SKIP LOCKED` and unique claim sequence constrain concurrent claims. Expiry, reclaim, and stale-worker behavior have SQL-verifier assertions but were not executed against the migration.
- **N4A-03 cancellation — source review PASS with external-call limitation:** Reopen and eligibility changes cancel pending/claimed parent work and clear claim/schedule fields. Unstarted attempts become `cancelled_before_start`; started attempts remain `started` with a cancellation timestamp so late provider evidence can be reconciled. Reconciliation does not make canceled work sendable. The external provider call cannot be atomically coupled to the database: a send already authorized/started may complete after cancellation, and its actual result is retained only if the restricted reconciliation mechanism receives evidence. This race is explicit, not eliminated.
- **N4A-04 manual accounting — source review PASS, runtime UNVERIFIED:** Reservation captures actor/time and immutable corrected recipient snapshot without changing attempt count/use fields. The `authorize...delivery` RPC is the durable `started` boundary; it atomically increments provider-attempt count and consumes the reserved manual attempt only with a current lease. Queue/claim/cancel-before-start leaves the recovery unconsumed, while cancellation remains terminal. The SQL verifier checks those paths but is not runnable against the current schema until the migration is applied.
- **Additional blocker — idempotency does not yet prove a stable provider payload:** Automatic claims do reuse the immutable parent `provider_idempotency_key` and copy the same original recipient assignment/group/profile IDs into each attempt. However, neither the notification nor attempt row stores a canonical email address/body/subject payload snapshot. The eventual worker would have to assemble fields (including mutable profile email/name and Session/Couple display data) from live records. The migration alone therefore cannot establish that retries send the exact same provider payload under the same key/window. Before worker implementation, persist a canonical versioned payload at a well-defined first-attempt boundary and reuse it unchanged for automatic retries, or explicitly design and verify an equivalent immutable source. Eligibility cancellation on relevant destination changes must be considered without silently changing the request payload.
- **Additional blocker — reconciliation evidence replay can produce inconsistent audit:** `reconcile_homework_completion_notification_attempt` permits an `uncertain` attempt to transition again. If the same provider event ID is first recorded as `uncertain` and later submitted as `accepted`/another outcome, the attempt and possibly parent state can change, but `homework_completion_notification_log` uses `ON CONFLICT(provider_event_id) DO NOTHING`; the append-only event history then retains only the earlier outcome. Define a duplicate-evidence rule: identical event/outcome is a no-op; conflicting outcome for the same event ID must be rejected, or a separately identified, audited superseding event must be required. Also reject blank `target_provider_message_id` when it is the only evidence identifier. Reconciliation remains service-role-only and non-sending, but its current evidence idempotency/audit semantics need correction before approval.
- **Automatic retry and terminal semantics — source review PASS, coverage incomplete:** The finalizer schedules retries after starts 1–4 at 5 minutes, 15 minutes, 1 hour, and 6 hours; start 5 becomes failed. Permanent rejection fails immediately; accepted/delivered become sent/delivered; uncertain is nonretryable; skipped is terminal; no eligible recipient is blocked; reopen/eligibility loss is canceled. Manual recovery can produce only one additional attempt after confirmed rejection. Same key and identity snapshot are stable at the DB field level; exact serialized payload and provider idempotency-window behavior are not verified because no provider worker exists.
- **Locking, grants, compatibility — static review:** Submission locks the shared `case_sessions` row before delegating to the existing paired-answer validation wrapper, so partner submissions serialize before cycle numbering/insertion; a unique `(case_session_id, completion_cycle)` constraint is the final duplicate guard. Worker claim uses `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`; state-changing worker RPCs lock parent then attempt, and reconciliation reads the parent ID without locking the attempt first, then locks parent then attempt. Cancellation also updates parent before attempt. No obvious lock-order inversion was found among those outbox transitions; external group/assignment/profile mutations can still race with the final eligibility check, so the documented in-flight boundary remains. The existing public persistence and reopen RPC signatures are retained; implementation delegates to the existing paired validation/reopen bodies and restores authenticated execution only on the wrapper. Tables have RLS, outbox/events are readable only to Admin/Super Admin, attempt table has no direct grants even to `service_role`, and service-only `SECURITY DEFINER` worker/reconcile RPCs use empty search paths and narrow grants. Manual recovery is authenticated Admin/Super Admin-only. This is source review only; catalog dependencies, effective grants, SQL compilation, and deployed compatibility have not been confirmed.
- **Rollback verifier coverage:** The current SQL verifier is one transaction ending in `ROLLBACK`, with synthetic Auth/Couple/Counselor/Case/Session/Homework records and simulated authenticated/service roles. It now covers first/second submissions, one cycle, reopen/resubmit, in-flight cancellation and late acceptance, recovery eligibility/reservation/cancel-before-start/start accounting, expired pre-start reclaim, expired started uncertainty, stale-finalizer denial, and RPC role boundaries. Owner-context assertions are outside `service_role` because direct table access is intentionally revoked. Gaps: it does not exercise the full five-attempt retry schedule or each delay, stable exact provider payload across retries, conflicting/duplicate reconciliation evidence, or genuinely simultaneous independent sessions. A single transaction cannot prove real lock contention.
- **DEV plan and runtime limits:** Do not apply until the two additional blockers above are resolved and the SQL is re-reviewed. After explicit apply approval, verify expected DEV ref `lctkqjjkhpyootwvttvj`; apply only this migration; run linked lint/history checks; run the rollback verifier and verify rollback plus zero synthetic residue. Separately, genuine concurrency needs a tagged synthetic fixture created in a committed setup transaction, two independent DB sessions released by a barrier to submit both partners / claim the same due set / reserve recovery, assertions for exactly one cycle/claim/reservation, then a tightly scoped cleanup transaction deleting only those IDs and a read-only zero-leftover check. This committed-fixture test requires explicit approval and must not touch real Cases. Actual provider idempotency/retry behavior requires a later controlled worker/DEV email milestone with synthetic recipients; no delivery can be tested in this DB-only migration review.
- **Tests, files, Git:** Focused migration-contract and reopen-action tests passed **14/14 across 2 files**; these are source-contract/action tests and do not compile or execute the migration. No typecheck/lint/build or SQL runtime verification was run for this review. This review changed only `PROJECT_STATE.md`; the existing migration, SQL verifier, and test remain uncommitted from N4A.3. Branch is `feature/homework-completion-notification`, HEAD `25bc4f3693aa8950eaa40ee94b6e10da77f0952e`; all eight stash object IDs remain unchanged. No AWHS change, commit, push, deployment, migration application, DB query/write, email, or PROD access occurred.
- **Next step:** Correct reconciliation duplicate-evidence semantics and establish a canonical immutable provider payload/retry contract (or explicitly defer payload guarantees until a reviewed worker design supplies it). Add tests for retry sequence/payload identity and evidence replay/conflict. Then conduct another pre-migration review before seeking approval to apply the foundation migration to DEV.

## N4A.4A Remaining Homework Notification Database Safeguards (November 19, 2026)

- **Status: IMPLEMENTED LOCALLY; migration remains unapplied and requires another independent pre-migration review.** Continued on `feature/homework-completion-notification`; preserved the existing N4A work. No DEV migration, query, data change, email, commit, push, deployment, PROD access, AWHS work, or stash operation occurred.
- **N4A-05 immutable provider payload:** Added a private, RLS-enabled, no-direct-grant payload table keyed uniquely by completion cycle plus delivery kind and by provider idempotency key. A service-role-only prepare RPC checks the live claim token and unexpired lease, validates the canonical request shape and recipient addresses against the claimed profile snapshot, rejects JSON fields intended for raw Homework answers, and freezes the full provider request before durable provider start. Subsequent automatic claims return the frozen request rather than accepting reconstructed content. The authorized start RPC requires that immutable payload, returns that exact payload with its key, and refuses provider start after the 24-hour Resend idempotency cutoff documented in the approved N-01 design. Manual recovery uses a distinct immutable payload/key only after the database has confirmed a permitted non-acceptance case. Profile-email changes cancel queued/claimed work if they invalidate eligibility or disagree with a frozen destination. The DB contract cannot semantically inspect HTML/text for leaked answer content; the future renderer must source only approved Couple/Session metadata and must have its own no-answer tests.
- **N4A-06 reconciliation evidence:** Added a private append-only provider-evidence table with unique provider-scoped normalized evidence identifiers. Reconciliation requires a nonblank event ID or message ID, rejects blank identifiers, inserts evidence and updates delivery state in one transaction, treats an identical replay as a no-op, and rejects reuse of an identifier for a different attempt/outcome/message/error. Distinct verified events can advance `uncertain → accepted → delivered` or `accepted → delivered`; invalid/regressive transitions are rejected. Canceled parent cycles remain canceled while attempt/evidence history can record late provider outcomes. Reconciliation remains service-role-only and cannot queue or send.
- **N4A-07 pre-delivery recovery:** Added a service-role-only pre-delivery failure RPC limited to a currently leased, not-started claim and verified `recipient_email_missing` or `recipient_email_invalid` conditions. It marks the claim as rejected before start, fails the parent without incrementing provider attempt count, and records no provider request. Admin/Super Admin recovery accepts this exact zero-start/single-pre-delivery-failure history only after both current assigned team members have valid emails and existing assignment, active membership, Homework submission, and role eligibility checks pass. The recovery receives a new key/payload and remains single-use; it is consumed only at durable provider start. Cancellation before that boundary remains terminal without consuming the recovery.
- **Retry and evidence regression coverage:** The rollback-only SQL verifier now prepares and reuses the same payload/key across all five automatic provider starts, asserts the 5-minute / 15-minute / 1-hour / 6-hour due intervals, and verifies attempt five is terminal. It also covers duplicate immutable-payload preparation with a changed candidate, canceled-delivery identical evidence replay, conflicting evidence rejection, missing evidence identifier rejection, distinct accepted-to-delivered progression, and missing-email pre-delivery recovery through corrected-email reservation and durable start. Synthetic fixtures remain wrapped by one `BEGIN … ROLLBACK`; this verifier has not been executed because the migration is intentionally unapplied. The test helper exposes only synthetic payload fixtures within the rollback verifier.

The linked DEV migration verifier, DB lint, and migration history were intentionally not run because DEV must not be queried or migrated in this task.
- **Concurrency and remaining risk:** Rollback SQL now exercises retry/payload/evidence/pre-delivery semantics sequentially. It does not prove concurrent recovery reservation or provider-evidence insert contention across independent sessions. Keep the previously documented approved procedure: provision uniquely tagged synthetic fixtures in a committed setup transaction, overlap calls through independent DEV sessions/barriers, assert one reservation/evidence row/claim, explicitly clean only those IDs, and verify zero residue. This requires a separately approved runtime verification window. ## N4A.4B Final Independent Homework Notification Database Review (November 19, 2026)

- **Overall migration readiness: BLOCKED / NOT READY TO APPLY.** This independent review inspected the actual unapplied migration, its RPC bodies, the rollback-only SQL verifier, and focused contract tests. The migration was not applied; no DEV query, data mutation, email, PROD access, commit, push, deployment, or stash operation occurred. Source review is not PostgreSQL compilation or runtime evidence.
- **N4A-01 — recovery eligibility: source PASS.** Recovery is limited to failed cycles whose started attempts are all explicitly `rejected_retryable` or `rejected_permanent`, or the narrowly permitted zero-start history containing exactly one verified missing/invalid-recipient pre-delivery rejection. Uncertain, accepted, delivered, bounced, canceled, mixed, and otherwise incomplete histories do not satisfy the predicates. The active Counselor-of-record and both current valid recipients are rechecked before reservation. The rollback verifier covers representative eligible/ineligible states sequentially; concurrent reservation remains unverified.
- **N4A-02 — leases: source PASS.** Prepare, pre-delivery failure, start, and finalization check parent/attempt claim identity and unexpired lease. Expired unstarted claims become `expired_before_start` and may be re-claimed with a new token; expired started claims become `uncertain` and are not automatically retried. Stale workers cannot finalize after expiry/reclaim. The verifier has assertions for these transitions but has not been run against PostgreSQL.
- **N4A-03 — cancellation: source PASS with the documented in-flight boundary.** Reopen and eligibility-loss paths cancel pending/claimed parent work and cancel unstarted attempts. A started attempt remains auditable with a cancellation timestamp; late provider evidence can update attempt history while the parent remains terminally canceled. This prevents future queueing/retry, but cannot retract a provider call already in flight. Cancellation/reconciliation behavior is covered sequentially in the rollback verifier, not at runtime.
- **N4A-04 — manual recovery accounting: source PASS.** Admin/Super Admin recovery is serialized by locking the parent notification. Reservation is single-use and does not consume a provider attempt; cancellation before start remains terminal and unconsumed. The service-role start RPC atomically assigns the attempt number, increments `attempt_count`, and records recovery use at the durable `started` boundary. Cross-session contention is not proven by a single rollback transaction.
- **N4A-05 — payload/key: source PARTIAL; release blocker.** The private payload table, immutable-row trigger, unique cycle/kind and key constraints, retry reuse, recipient check, and rejection of explicit answer-related JSON keys are present. However, the implementation sets `idempotency_cutoff_at` to `clock_timestamp() + interval '24 hours'` when the payload is prepared (migration around line 1038), before the first provider request. This is not a conservative cutoff inside a 24-hour provider window: the provider's retention window starts when it receives the first request, so preparation-to-send delay can extend retries beyond that window and defeat duplicate protection. The contract test currently asserts this exact 24-hour value, so it codifies rather than catches the issue. Shorten/derive the cutoff with an explicit safety margin that accounts for the gap to first send, and ensure the future worker observes that boundary before every provider request; add a regression asserting the cutoff is strictly inside the provider window. The database can reject answer-named JSON keys but cannot establish that free-form `html`/`text` contains no answer content; N4B/N4C must render only approved Couple/Session metadata and test that no answers enter the payload. This content-source boundary remains a required downstream privacy gate.
- **N4A-06 — evidence: source PASS for identity/replay integrity; retry semantic gap needs resolution.** Evidence requires a nonblank event or message identifier, is append-only and unique per provider/evidence key, is inserted atomically with outcome updates, treats exact replay as a no-op, and rejects reuse with conflicting attempt/event/message/outcome/error data. Distinct events can advance uncertain/accepted delivery states; canceled parents remain canceled. Additional finding: when reconciliation reports a verified `rejected_retryable` outcome for an uncertain/started attempt, the function sets the parent to `failed` rather than scheduling the approved next automatic retry. The ordinary leased finalizer does schedule the four backoffs. If webhook/reconciliation is an accepted path for learning transient rejection, this creates inconsistent N-01D behavior and leaves a confirmed retryable failure for manual recovery. Before approval, define whether such evidence should atomically schedule the same remaining automatic retry (without sending in reconciliation) or be classified terminal; add the corresponding test. Current SQL verifier tests replay/conflict/blank IDs and accepted-to-delivered progression, but not retryable rejection reconciliation.
- **N4A-07 — pre-delivery recovery: source PASS for missing/invalid email.** The restricted service-role failure RPC requires a live unexpired, unstarted claim and verifies the named missing/invalid condition against the current recipient profile. It records `rejected_before_start` without incrementing provider attempts. Admin recovery only accepts the exact single pre-delivery history after both current assigned recipients have valid addresses, then uses a separate key and consumes its one attempt only at durable start. The rollback verifier covers the missing-email correction path; invalid-email and actual SQL behavior remain unverified.
- **Additional source checks:** The normal finalizer encodes delays of 5 minutes, 15 minutes, 1 hour, and 6 hours after starts 1–4 and fails after start 5. Uncertain outcomes are not automatically requeued. Payload/evidence tables have RLS enabled and direct grants revoked (including service_role); service-only payload/worker/reconciliation RPCs are narrowly granted and use `SECURITY DEFINER` with empty search paths. Admin/Super Admin can read only parent/event history through RLS; attempts, payload, and evidence have no direct grants. Parent/attempt state changes generally acquire parent then attempt locks; `SKIP LOCKED` and unique constraints protect claims/cycles. Existing submit/reopen public signatures remain and delegate to the existing validation/authorization bodies. No obvious static lock-order inversion or change to existing answer validation was found. These conclusions require catalog/runtime confirmation after an approved DEV apply.
- **Rollback verifier coverage and gaps:** `supabase/tests/homework_paired_response_runtime.sql` is self-contained with synthetic Auth/Couple/Counselor/Case/Session/Homework fixtures in a transaction ending in `ROLLBACK`. It covers paired submission/reopen compatibility, roles, cancellation, recovery, stale leases, payload reuse, the four delays/five starts, evidence replay/conflict/blank ID, and missing-email recovery. It does not establish actual SQL compilation, transaction rollback/zero residue, true simultaneous submits/claims/recovery, invalid-email recovery, service-role direct table denial for each private table, retryable reconciliation scheduling, or provider delivery/idempotency behavior. The Vitest suite is source-string contract coverage, not a PostgreSQL parser or runtime test.
- **Validation and Git:** `npx vitest run features/counseling-sessions/homework-completion-notification-migration.test.ts features/counseling-sessions/homework-reopen-actions.test.ts` passed **17/17** tests. `git diff --check` passed. No SQL verifier, DB lint, migration alignment, typecheck, or PostgreSQL compilation was run for this audit. Branch remains `feature/homework-completion-notification` at `25bc4f3693aa8950eaa40ee94b6e10da77f0952e`. The pre-existing uncommitted migration, SQL verifier, and contract test were preserved; this section is the only additional file edit. All eight stash hashes remain unchanged: `684f8f76c4cd019854bf83bca8e928bfe9646532`, `88a2440fad42752f76d507aba3c03ba2c56f892a`, `441cff42446e3efb2cfea35d163f603a9b66b990`, `e3b498f81cb644ea6b5d78b15d9df74c3b06231b`, `b7c3cda01e73ebc0a88835b9bfab6bde67a1d71d`, `16dd8164ed400426bbab7ef330c66448b564faad`, `6ca55c504d83b82de5a0ab88f444279f21a14aa3`, and `697b7f009140f90e54b968ebb4a9d957641cb434`.
- **Exact next step:** Correct the cutoff to a conservative pre-expiry boundary and settle/test retryable evidence behavior. Then repeat independent migration review. ## N4A.4C Final Cutoff and Reconciliation Corrections (November 19, 2026)

- **Status: implemented locally; migration remains unapplied.** This update supersedes N4A.4B's two source-review blockers for cutoff timing and reconciled transient rejections. Work remains on `feature/homework-completion-notification`; no DEV access, migration application, email, PROD access, AWHS work, stash operation, commit, push, deployment, or merge occurred.
- **N4A-05 cutoff:** The private immutable payload now leaves `first_provider_started_at` and `idempotency_cutoff_at` null during preparation. The first authorized durable provider-start transaction atomically binds the timestamp and a fixed `+23 hours` cutoff; a constraint and mutation trigger prevent later change. Automatic retries reuse the same payload/key and cutoff. The start RPC rejects a new start at or beyond the stored cutoff and returns the cutoff and first-start timestamp for the future sender to check again immediately before its external request. Preparation delay therefore does not consume the idempotency window. A first-start transaction only becomes durable on commit; the future worker must not perform HTTP delivery unless the RPC succeeded and the returned cutoff is still in the future at the last pre-request check.
- **N4A-06 reconciled transient rejection:** Reconciliation records verified rejection evidence atomically with attempt state. A retryable rejection on an automatic attempt returns the parent to `pending` only when the attempt-specific next delay exists, the retry due time is before the immutable cutoff, current Counselor/HW eligibility still holds, and the parent is not canceled. Attempt count, recipient, payload, and provider key are unchanged; the next start increments the normal attempt number. The schedule remains 5 minutes, 15 minutes, 1 hour, and 6 hours after attempts one through four, with at most five automatic starts. Rejections that exhaust attempts or cutoff become `failed`; canceled work remains terminal. An exact replay of the same evidence returns the already-current state without rescheduling; conflicting reuse is rejected. Unresolved `uncertain` outcomes remain non-sendable until reconciliation supplies verified evidence.

## N4A.5C.1 Disposable Local Supabase Environment Assessment (November 19, 2026)

- **Overall: FEASIBLE IN PRINCIPLE; BLOCKED before setup or migration replay.** The host is Apple Silicon macOS (`Darwin 27.0.0 arm64`, macOS 27.0), which exceeds Supabase's documented macOS 14+ platform floor for its experimental native process runtime. No Docker or other container runtime is installed, as intended. Official documentation describes native runtime as running Supabase services as host processes and explicitly supports Apple silicon macOS; the runtime is experimental and its interface/compatibility may change ([native runtime guide](https://supabase.com/docs/guides/local-development/docker-and-native-runtimes)).
- **CLI prerequisite:** Installed Supabase CLI is **2.117.0**. The multiple-local-projects guide requires **2.119.0+** and enabling the experimental stack feature. However, this repository has `pg_cron` scheduling migrations, and the native-runtime guide states macOS native `pg_cron` jobs fail before CLI **2.121.0**. Therefore **2.121.0 is the minimum practical version for this project**, not merely the general 2.119.0 floor. No CLI upgrade was performed or approved. `SUPABASE_EXPERIMENTAL_STACK=1` exposes stack commands only on a compatible CLI; the current installed CLI does not expose them.
- **Local setup state:** `supabase/config.toml` is absent. The existing local link metadata still identifies hosted DEV as `lctkqjjkhpyootwvttvj`; it was not changed. Host `tar` is available; Homebrew `libpq` client tools (`psql`, `pg_isready`) are present, but server binaries (`postgres`, `initdb`, `pg_ctl`) are absent. No native stack was initialized or started. Supabase documents that native runtime downloads and checksum-verifies service archives on first start, stores local stack data under `~/.supabase/stacks/<id>/`, and shares a service cache under `~/.supabase/cache/stack`. This requires network access to the documented artifact hosts, but no hosted database or paid service.
- **Migration and extension compatibility:** Supabase documents the native runtime as the same local Supabase services and service versions as its container runtime; that makes replay of this project's 177 migrations plausible, including PostgreSQL extensions, but compatibility is **not verified**. In particular, migration success, actual PostgreSQL version/extension availability, roles/RPC behavior, local lint, and rollback verifier results require a disposable local stack and remain unverified. The installed CLI's cached PostgreSQL image metadata is not evidence of native runtime compatibility.
- **Isolation and safe-disposal assessment:** The native runtime uses local host processes, not containers. Project identity incorporates repo root, Git branch, and optional stack name; local data is separate from hosted projects, but multiple native projects share host process/file-lock resources, so Supabase recommends one native project per environment. Use an explicit native runtime and local-only database commands/localhost connection; never use `--linked`/remote commands or copy hosted data. Stack destruction removes that local stack's database and settings while retaining the shared download cache. This is appropriate for immutable synthetic history because disposal removes the entire synthetic database rather than deleting protected event rows. **The disposal path itself has not been exercised.**
- **Important migration safety blocker:** Existing `20261016100000_schedule_meeting_reminders.sql` and `20261105140000_schedule_onboarding_reminders.sql` create `pg_cron` jobs that issue `net.http_get` using Vault endpoint/secret values. The new notification foundation migration creates no worker schedule, but replaying all 177 migrations locally could activate unrelated reminder schedules or fail if required local Vault values are missing. Before reset/replay, a safe, tested local-only way to supply non-production values and prevent any outbound request must be established. No real secrets, endpoints, HTTP requests, emails, or jobs were created in this assessment. Do not proceed with migration replay until this guard is verified.
- **No external changes:** This turn did not access hosted DEV or PROD, run a database query, start a database, replay migrations, create test data, send email/provider requests, or change environment/link configuration. Before reconciliation, a service-role-only verifier checks that the attempt belongs to the tagged notification, the provider message ID is consistent, and the recipient and immutable payload tags match the frozen payload. A mismatch is acknowledged without changing state; verifier or reconciliation RPC errors fail closed. Existing accepted/delivered/bounced state mapping was preserved. The lookup/correlation migration is `20261119100000_homework_completion_notification_attempt_lookup.sql`; it is unapplied and has static contract coverage only. It has not been compiled or executed against DEV.
- **Single-recipient source guard:** The template rejects blank or multiple `to` addresses and never supplies `cc` or `bcc`. The delivery parser rejects payloads with anything other than one address. Delivery also checks the resolved current assignment recipient snapshot before payload preparation or provider send. These checks prevent unintended multi-recipient sends but cannot resolve the database-contract conflict above.
