## N-01 Two Assigned Counselor Recipients (November 19, 2026)

- **Approved behavior:** When the active assigned team has two active, individually eligible Counselors, both receive a separate Homework completion email. A Session still has one completion-cycle record. Each Counselor's delivery, Resend message, idempotency key, retry schedule, cancellation/eligibility, webhook reconciliation, and one-time manual recovery must be tracked independently. A successful delivery to one partner must never be repeated because the other failed.
- **Implementation status:** Recipient-level database contracts are implemented locally in the new, unapplied `20261119120000_homework_completion_recipient_deliveries.sql`. The previously applied `20261118100000_homework_completion_notification_foundation.sql` is untouched. Delivery remains safely inactive: the old cycle-level worker claim is revoked, and the current application worker has not yet been adapted to child delivery RPCs.
- **Database design implemented:** `homework_completion_notifications` remains one unique per-Session completion cycle. New private child rows in `homework_completion_notification_deliveries` are unique per cycle and recipient. Attempts, payloads, keys, leases, outcomes, and one-time recovery are scoped to the child. The migration adapts enqueue, claim, payload preparation, durable provider start, finish, recovery, cancellation, webhook verification, and evidence reconciliation. Parent status is an aggregate for existing Admin summaries. Reopening and recipient eligibility loss cancel only unsent recipient rows; started attempt outcomes remain recordable on their own history without restoring canceled delivery eligibility. The attempt-lookup migration is superseded at runtime by the final recipient-aware function definitions in this later migration; neither migration is applied by this task.
- **Delivery cases to cover:** two eligible Counselors (two separate provider calls/keys); one eligible Counselor (one send); none eligible (blocked cycle, no send); partial accepted/delivered versus failed/retryable; recipient reassignment before claim/start; reopen then new completion cycle; one recipient's recovery without retrying the successful delivery; signed event mismatch/replay/conflict; and service-role-only direct-access denial. No live provider request was made. Genuine CC-01–07 multi-session concurrency remains deferred/unverified.
- **Scheduling:** The once-per-minute Vercel cron entry was removed from local `vercel.json` so the feature does not depend on an unverified Vercel plan or require a plan upgrade. Existing DEV database migrations already use `pg_cron` plus `pg_net` to call protected Vercel cron routes hourly. The supported path for the required short retry delays is a dedicated one-minute `pg_cron` job using the existing Vault endpoint/cron-secret pattern, targeting `/api/cron/homework-completion-notifications`; it must be defined in a reviewed forward migration and is not active until separately approved/applied. Existing meeting/onboarding schedules must remain unchanged. Verify the deployed route, Vault secret names/values by safe identity checks, and active job cadence before authorizing activation.
- **Validation at the earlier decision point:** The prior 35-test run covered the then-current single-recipient fail-closed implementation; the current recipient-level migration has separate focused source-contract coverage below. Neither run is a database execution or live email verification.

Its finding that the applied schema is cycle-scoped and cannot represent independent recipient delivery remains valid. The earlier exact-one template/delivery guards are temporary fail-closed safeguards and must be replaced by child-delivery-specific single-recipient validation before implementation can be considered complete.

No migration was applied, no live email/provider request or hosted configuration change occurred, and there was no commit, push, merge, deployment, or PROD access. `vercel.json` now retains only the existing daily keep-alive cron entry.

## N-01 Recipient-Level Database Delivery Implementation (November 19, 2026)

- **Status: IMPLEMENTED LOCALLY; NOT APPLIED.** On `feature/homework-completion-notification`, authored the forward migration `supabase/migrations/20261119120000_homework_completion_recipient_deliveries.sql`. The applied foundation migration remains unchanged. This change does not send mail, call a provider, modify hosted DEV/PROD, apply migrations, deploy, commit, or push.
- **Parent and child model:** One row in `homework_completion_notifications` remains the unique completion-cycle record. New private `homework_completion_notification_deliveries` rows are unique by `(notification_id, recipient_profile_id)` and each has a recipient-specific stable key, independent status/retry counter, lease, cancellation state, and manual-recovery audit fields. Seed logic creates one or two child rows for the active assigned operational team members meeting the existing role/activity eligibility rules; zero eligible members leave the parent blocked with no sendable child. Existing two-member team and one active Counselor-of-record invariants are retained.
- **Recipient-scoped RPC contracts:** `claim_due_homework_completion_recipient_deliveries` uses parent/child row locks and `SKIP LOCKED`; `prepare_homework_completion_recipient_payload` freezes one-recipient payload per child/key; `authorize_homework_completion_recipient_delivery` rechecks eligibility and binds the immutable 23-hour cutoff at durable start; `finish_homework_completion_recipient_attempt` finalizes one child and schedules only that child's approved retry; `request_homework_completion_recipient_recovery` reserves the single Admin/Super Admin recovery for that child; `reconcile_homework_completion_recipient_attempt` records idempotent provider evidence for that attempt. Existing lookup and webhook verifier signatures are replaced with delivery-aware implementations. Old cycle claim and cycle recovery grants are disabled, and direct invocation of the renamed legacy reconciler is revoked; the wrapper can still dispatch legacy attempts internally.
- **Lifecycle and safety:** Reopening/reassignment cancels eligible pending/claimed child deliveries without rewriting sent outcomes. A provider outcome arriving for a previously started but canceled child updates only the attempt history and does not re-enable sending. Expired leases remain fail-closed; expired started attempts after cancellation become uncertain for history only. Automatic attempt count remains capped at five and distinct from the one manual recovery; manual recovery does not consume the automatic count. Payloads and keys are delivery-specific, attempt links are constrained to the same parent cycle, recipient payload is checked against one current profile email, and no CC/BCC is accepted. Private delivery data has RLS enabled and no direct role grants.
- **Tests/verifier added:** `features/counseling-sessions/homework-recipient-delivery-migration.test.ts` provides local SQL contract coverage for one cycle, one/two recipient cardinality, per-recipient uniqueness, locks/leases, retries, cancellation, recovery, webhook/reconciliation, and role restrictions. `supabase/tests/homework_completion_recipient_delivery_contract_runtime.sql` is a read-only transaction-scoped post-migration catalog/privilege/function-contract verifier. It does not create fixtures or execute recipient state transitions. The existing full `homework_paired_response_runtime.sql` verifier is still cycle-scoped and must be adapted before it can verify the new child worker functions; no claim is made that it passes after this unapplied migration.
- **Validation:** Focused tests passed **41 tests across 6 files** (recipient migration contract, foundation/lookup migration contracts, worker, template, provider/webhook). `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint`, `npm run build`, and `git diff --check` passed. Typecheck was rerun after the production build completed because the first parallel run raced with `.next/types` regeneration. No SQL parser, PostgreSQL server, or local database is installed; the new migration and post-migration SQL verifier have not been compiled or executed. No DEV runtime or authorization simulation was performed for the new schema. Genuine CC-01–07 concurrency verification remains unverified.
- **Compatibility gate:** Current N4B worker and Admin recovery are still cycle-scoped and expect the old claim/prepare/start/finish/recovery RPCs. This migration revokes the old cycle claim and recovery entry points intentionally so the old worker cannot send one message to a two-person `to` list or mutate the wrong recipient. Therefore do not apply this migration or activate a schedule until the immediate follow-up updates worker/webhook/Admin actions to the new recipient-scoped APIs and runs mocked integration tests. The pending `20261119100000_homework_completion_notification_attempt_lookup.sql` remains unchanged; this later migration redefines its lookup/webhook helpers after creating delivery rows.

Changes remain uncommitted; no AWHS changes, hosted operations, migration application, emails, provider calls, commits, pushes, merges, or deployments occurred. The migration is not yet release-ready for DEV application until static validation is complete and application integration is updated.

The signed webhook route requires a valid notification, delivery, and attempt ID plus exactly one recipient and a provider message ID. It calls the updated service-only correlation RPC, which binds the event to the same notification, recipient delivery, frozen one-recipient payload, attempt, and provider message ID before invoking the existing evidence reconciler. Duplicate event replays are still delegated to the database's idempotent evidence handling; mismatched recipients/deliveries are acknowledged without changing another child's state. Failed/uncertain items identify the Counselor by display name without exposing email or provider payload; no-eligible-Counselor cycle-level blocked notices remain visible without a recovery action. The one-time recovery action accepts a delivery ID and calls `request_homework_completion_recipient_recovery`, targeting only that recipient record. The database retains the authoritative role, eligibility, evidence, and one-attempt checks.
- **Forward migration contracts adjusted locally:** Added the two narrowly scoped read RPCs to the existing unapplied recipient-delivery migration and updated webhook verification to require the delivery ID. The service-only context RPC returns only current recipient email, Couple display names/genders, Session metadata, and IDs for the active claim; it does not return Homework answers. The alert RPC is authenticated-only, independently checks Admin/Super Admin role, and omits Counselor email, payload, and answer data. Tight RPC grants are documented in the migration and post-migration contract verifier. The applied foundation migration was not edited.
